### Chapter 5: Box of Evil

he scout-investigators find themselves deep in a mystery when curiosity about T an odd box belonging to Colonel Grimm places them in peril. Playable by up to six scoutinvestigators, it takes approximately one or two sessions to complete.

### Keeper Information

Colonel Josef Grimm first encountered the secretive fraternal society, the **Sons of Seth** (page 60), at a grand dinner in Cairo, Egypt. He was accustomed to attending such affairs, due to his celebrity as a renowned adventurer and explorer. Grimm would enjoy his host’s hospitality in exchange for telling his outrageous stories.

Intrigued by the secret society, Grimm accepted their invitation. The Sons of Seth is an international organization, with new groups springing up around the globe.

When he had the opportunity to join the temple in his hometown of Westhaven he jumped, secretly aspiring to becoming its leader. So, Grimm held masked balls and gatherings, secretly courting scions of Westhaven’s wealthy families with the intention of recruiting them. While most view the sect’s secrets and ceremonies as quaint or thrilling, only a few know the real inner workings. Now in his 70s, Grimm yearns for a quieter life, but has no wish to retire.

Town council vice president, **Thomas Cline** (page 60), was the original founder of the Westhaven Temple of Seth. Cline became most aggrieved when Grimm took over and has nursed a grudge ever since.

Spotting an opportunity for revenge, Cline arranged for one of Grimm’s packages to be intercepted and replaced with a dangerous trap.

Grimm believes he is receiving a gemstone artifact that allows divination of the future. The package actually contains a wicker box, which hides a homunculus. When freed, the creature has been instructed to command the mummy on display in Grimm’s lounge to kill everyone in the house—starting with Grimm!

Cline plans to clean up any loose ends and retrieve the wicker box later. He knows authorities will conclude some sort of burglary occurred which ended in tragedy.

### Involving the Scout-investigators

One or more scout-investigators observes a fancy black car pull up to Colonel Grimm’s house. Two men in dark suits unload a moderately sized wooden crate from the back of the car. One appears to, strangely, say something to the crate, and then waves his hands over it in a weird manner. It’s all most curious. The two men take the crate inside Grimm’s house and exit several minutes later, empty-handed, and depart.

Everyone knows Colonel Grimm’s house is full of amazing and exotic artifacts. The idea of getting a peek at his latest prize should pique the scoutinvestigator’s interest. If desired, perhaps a scoutinvestigator could be related to Colonel Grimm.

- **Playing as a stand-alone scenario:** any scoutinvestigators can be used.
- **Playing as part two of the Westhaven Campaign:** two years have passed since **Tremors Below** (page 70), and it’s now 1920 (the exact date is unimportant); scout-investigators should now be **Rovers** (page 29).


### Chapter 5

### Dramatis Personae

**Note: Box of Evil** takes place two years after **Tremors Below** , and so recurring NPC’s ages have been increased accordingly.


Colonel Josef Grimm, _**age 74 **_ **,** _**renowned adventurer**_ Colonel Grimm returned to his quiet hometown of Westhaven 10 years ago. He claimed it was because family lived nearby, although those who met Grimm considered him a loner rather than a family man.

Grimm is a renowned world traveler and adventurer, and though he dislikes the word “retired,” his exploring days are behind him. He once drove sled dogs across Siberia in search of long lost ancient northern kingdoms, scaled mountains deemed too dangerous to climb, and collected exotic artifacts—most of which now adorn his Westhaven mansion.

Now, he divides his time between writing adventure stories and leading the Westhaven Temple of the Sons of Seth. His wild stories are serialized in popular monthly magazines with a nationwide audience. He claims all are true. This includes his encounter with a strange, hairy beast high in the Himalayas; and the time he journeyed deep into Africa, caught malaria, and suffered fever dreams of a ziggurat where snake people conducted dark rituals. Such nightmares haunt him to this day.

Perhaps his greatest adventures were in the land of the pharaohs. There, he explored the Nile Valley and became obsessed with ancient Egyptian beliefs.

Grimm’s life is full of routine these days. He wakes, eats breakfast, writes, has afternoon tea, eats supper, and then goes to bed. This routine only varies when he entertains guests, goes out for meetings, or attends the sect’s temple. He never married; he only shares the house with his long-time housekeeper, Mrs. Habersham, and his trusty hound, Buford.

- **Description:** an older gentleman, smartly dressed, with white thinning hair; healthy and strong for his age.
- **Traits:** smokes a pipe, which he waves around when speaking.
- **Roleplaying Hooks:** little patience with meddling kids, but entertains fans of his writing or those wishing to hear about his adventures.


**Evelyn Habersham,** _**age 57**_ **,** _**housekeeper**_ Mrs. Habersham is Colonel Grimm’s dutiful housekeeper. She sees to his daily needs with a bright and positive personality, even when put-upon by one of Grimm’s frequent moods.


### Box of Evil

- **Description:** short and pudgy, with gray hair; always wears a house apron over a plain dress.
- **Traits:** fond of children and tends toward leniency should scout-investigators misbehave.
- **Roleplaying Hooks:** she may aid scout-investigators fighting the mummy but is likely to become another victim.


### **buford,** _**age 10**_ **,** _**trusty Hound**_

Grimm’s old hunting dog is a friendly black Labrador Retriever whose hunting days are long behind him.

- **Description:** a stocky, black Labrador Retriever, 85 pounds, with gray hair around his chin and eyes.
- **• Traits:** loyal, protective of Grimm.
- **Roleplaying Hooks:** following his owner’s death, Buford needs a new home—should they wish, a scout-investigator could adopt him.

### robert “bob” Harley, _**age 40 **_ **,** _**purveyor of Sweet Treats**_ for Bob Harley‘s Description, See Page 57.

### **amanda Harley,** _**age 13**_ **,** _**shop Assistant**_

Amanda is Bob Harley’s daughter. She frequently works in her father’s shop after school. She knows all the scout-investigators from school and is friends

### Leads: Obscure & Obvious Clues

At the end of key sections, we list “leads” to help Keepers run scenarios smoothly. This provides clear routes for scout-investigators to follow in terms of clues and plot progression. The leads tell Keepers which scene or event comes next, navigating the path the story can take from here.

- If the lead is labeled “obvious”—make scoutinvestigators aware of it as the scene progresses. They usually do not require a skill roll. Obvious leads are essential to the plot.
- If the lead is labeled “obscure”—the clue usually requires some form of specific action or a skill roll to access the information; or it may be available from a more easily accessible alternative source. Usually, obscure clues are not necessary to move the scenario forward.

with many. At the Keeper’s discretion, she could be smitten with a scout-investigator, providing them extra toppings for free.

**Option:** Amanda Harley can be played as a scout -investigator.

### Start: Let’s Get a Soda

The scenario begins at Harley’s Sweet Shoppe (page 56), where the scout-investigators have met for a soda after the weekly squad meeting. One way or another, they should all learn about Grimm’s mysterious delivery (see **Keeper Information**, page 89)—ideally, one or more scout-investigators saw the strange delivery and report it to their squad now.

- If the delivery was witnessed by a scout-investigator, give them **Handout: Box 1** ; invite them to present their account of events.
- Otherwise, the delivery to Grimm was witnessed by **Amanda Harley** (see **Dramatis Personae** , nearby), and she is eager to tell the scout-investigators all about it—in this case, read aloud or paraphrase **Handout: Box 1** to the group.


### Chapter 5

### Boxcar Jim

As they leave Harley’s Sweet Shoppe, **Boxcar Jim** (page 62) tosses pebbles at the scouts to get their attention. His eyes look wild; his graying beard—and most of his face—has a coat of vanilla ice cream from a discarded container he found in the trash.

> _“Oh, hey there, kiddos. Off on any exciting adventures today? Say, you don’t have a few pennies for your old pal Jim, do ya? I woke up under the Third Street Bridge, and I felt like I was burning. I had a terrible dream… that I was on fire! The whole world was burning. That’s why I’m eating this ice cream, so I can cool down! But if you have a few pennies, I can get a cool drink someplace._

> _“Can you help old Jim out? Ya know, if you’re going camping sometime soon, I have a neat trick to help start a campfire… if you have a few pennies for old Jim.”_

If the scouts part with a few coins or a cool drink for Jim, he hands them a crumpled napkin. Then, he says, “ _Say the words written on the napkin, and—poof!—a fire starts!”_ **Keeper note:** the spell on Jim’s napkin is **Immolate** (see **Boxcar Jim’s Useful Items** , page 63).

As they finish talking, Bob Harley comes out and shouts at Jim to move on and stop harassing customers! Jim tells the scouts, _“Best of luck!”_ and runs off down an alley.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** scout-investigators may wish to figure out who owns **The Mysterious Black Car** (following).

- Obvious: if witnesses to the delivery know Thomas Cline (page 60), they recognize him—if they pursue this lead, see **Investigating Cline** (page 61).

- **Obvious:** proceed to **Colonel Grimm’s Mansion** (page 93.)

### The Mysterious Black Car

The car delivering the package to Grimm belongs to Thomas Cline (page 60). Scout-investigators who saw the car make the delivery automatically recognize it when it arrives at the of end of the scenario (see **Wrapping Things Up**, page 109).

Optionally, should scout-investigators look for the car, **Spot Hidden** may spy it driving past, Thomas Cline leering out at them, ominously.


### Box of Evil

### Colonel Grimm’s Mansion

Colonel Grimm’s mansion (# 17 on the **Westhaven Map**, page 54) is a stately, two-story colonial-style home located on a wealthy block near downtown Westhaven. The large plot boasts a beautiful garden, tended by hired staff. The property sits back off the main street with a jug-handle driveway; huge, evergreen arborvitae obscure most of the building from the street.

Grimm’s mansion is a shrine to the turn-of-thecentury concept of manliness embodied by the likes of Theodore Roosevelt, Sitting Bull, and Buffalo Bill Cody. Every hallway and room feature artifacts from Grimm’s journeys. These items range from strange to downright terrifying—nothing mundane comes into this house!

### Using Stealth

In essence, scout-investigators have two ways to creep into the mansion:

- Use **Stealth** to sneak in via an open door or window.
- Use **Mechanical Repair** or a Hard **STR** roll to break in.
- Create a distraction (or wait for one) and use that to dart in while the housekeeper is occupied.
- Or use a **Luck** roll to determine how quickly a “naturally occurring” distraction, such as the mail or other delivery, happens: success means sometime in the next hour, while failure means waiting for 1D4+1 hours.

Of course, if scout-investigators create their own distraction, no Luck roll is needed. However, Keepers may call for a **Charm** , **Fast Talk** , or **Persuade** roll from the person causing the distraction to ensure Mrs. Habersham remains occupied while their friends slip inside.

If caught, Mrs. Habersham expels the scoutinvestigators and sends them on their way.

**Note:** accessing the mansion is easy if one of the scoutinvestigators is related to Grimm, such as his great nephew or niece. Failing that, scout-investigators must rely on secrecy and stealth—see **Using Stealth** , following.


**CHAPTER 5**

### Inside the Mansion

### First Floor

### Entrance Hall

An exquisite marble-tiled floor leads the way into to this beautiful house. Several oil paintings of Colonel Grimm hang from the walls; while a balcony is visible, no stairs can be seen from here.

A pair of doors, currently open, lead into the interior of the mansion. On either side of these doors stand two terrifyingly massive stuffed bears, both standing 10 feet (3 m) tall in attack poses. The bear on the left is a brown, grizzly bear, while a polar bear is on the right.

### Living Room

Numerous stuffed birds adorn the sitting room walls. Some stand on perches crafted to look like trees, while others are attached to walls, as in flight. In addition, several large potted plants are stationed around. The wallpaper is jungle themed creating the illusion that one is in the middle of a dense jungle.

A leather couch and wooden coffee table stand to one side while three matching armchairs sit on the other side of the room. Next to one armchair is a cart with a silver tea set. An exotic rug of vine-like designs pulls the design together.

### Kitchen

In the center of the room is a preparation table, with butcher’s block and an icebox nearby. Cast-iron cookware hangs from one wall, while a set of kitchen knives rest in a wooden block. Various cupboards and drawers contain food and earthenware. A basket of firewood sits beside a cast-iron stove. If near mealtime, **Evelyn Habersham** (page 90), the housekeeper, can usually be found in here.

### Dining Room

This large, elegant room is perpetually set for an engagement, even though formal dinners are rare. Display cases show off pottery pieces and clay artifacts from Grimm’s travels. The silverware and serving set on the huge dining table gleams. Two windows look out to the backyard, while a door provides access outside.

### Library

Bookshelves line the walls, holding an impressive and expansive collection. A reading table stands in the center with two comfortable leather chairs. The books cover a wide subject range: art history and appreciation, foreign travel, medicine, philosophy, US and world politics, world history, and so on.

**Keeper note:** the silverware was a gift from an Indian Maharaja near Punjab, so Grimm claims.

**95** **CHAPTER 5**

### Study

Grimm’s inner sanctum. All his most prized possessions can be found in here. An old oak desk dominates, while bookshelves line the walls. Display cases feature some of the rarest, most valuable artifacts Grimm collected over his travels. Taking pride of place is an 8 foot tall (2.4 m) Egyptian sarcophagus, ornately decorated with colorful paintwork and flecks of gold inlay.

The bookshelves contain numerous journals written by Grimm during his lifetime of adventures, as well as dozens of rolled-up maps. A gun rack, with a 12-gauge double-barreled shotgun (unloaded) clings to one wall.

- Inside the study (and viewable from the window) is the wooden packing crate, mysteriously delivered earlier.

**Keeper note:** if asked about the sarcophagus, Grimm says, _“Why, that’s old Ramy! He is an old-timer and mostly just naps these days._ ” Then, he lets out a loud _“Ha!”_ clearly thinking himself quite witty. For more on what’s inside the study, see **Inside Grimm’s Study** , page 97.

### Second Floor

### Guest Bedroom 2

With a double bed, nightstand, and matching dresser. Instead of taxidermy, here are exotic flowering plants in ceramic pots.

### Getting into Grimm’s Study

Asked for permission to enter his study, Grimm exclaims, _“Harrumph, my study is not a place for children. It contains many delicate and expensive artifacts. I will not have you wrecking them with your grubby little hands. Now, go play ball outside.”_ If a scout-investigator can think of a valid reason to enter the study, allow a **Charm** or **Fast Talk** roll; with success, Grimm believes the story and allows them inside; although another scout may need to distract him while the others inspect the crate.

### _**sneaking In**_

If scout-investigators try to sneak into the house (by any means):

### Main Bedroom

Colonel Grimm’s private bedroom contains a four-poster bed and matching end tables, as well as two full armoires and an oversized armchair. A bull elephant’s head hangs ominously above the bed, while several old vases sport artwork reminiscent of the early Chinese dynasties. The walls are adorned with taxidermy and art.

### Bathroom

An elegant affair with white porcelain and silver fixtures. A claw-foot tub takes up most of the space. White and black tiles cover the floor.

### Mrs. Habersham’s Bedroom

With double bed, dresser, wardrobe, and bedside tables, this room has a more feminine aspect. However, the two huge, stuffed marabou stork in the corners are somewhat out of place; they offer a potential fright for anyone encountering them in the dark!

### Guest Bedroom 1

With a queen-sized bed, matching nightstand, dresser, and armoire. Two oil paintings of the African savannah and some small stuffed birds are affixed to the walls.

- Ask for a group **Luck** roll:
- **Success:** they get into the study without being noticed.
- **Failure:** they must contend with Mrs. Habersham doing chores or Colonel Grimm; a successful Stealth roll gets them to the study—or they could try to cause a diversion.
- If they get caught, Grimm or Habersham likely chases the scouts out of the house, unless they provide a convincing excuse (e.g., “we followed a burglar in,” etc.). A **Charm** , **Fast Talk** , or **Persuade** roll determines if their excuse works. In this case, the scouts go to the kitchen for milk and cookies, while Grimm searches the house (or whatever’s appropriate). On finding no burglar, Grimm thanks the scouts for their vigilance, then sends them on their way. They must try again.
- If Grimm catches a relative whom he previously denied permission to enter the study, they suffer the full weight of his wrath: immediate ejection. They must try again.


### Box of Evil

### _**breaking In**_

If nothing else works, sneaking into the study via a window may be the only remaining option (a successful **DEX** roll opens the latch). Failing that, scouts could break a pane to open the latch. However, they need to time events so Grimm is not close enough to hear.

large wicker box with a brass latch secured with an intricate copper-wire braid. Beside the box is a letter, an open journal, and some loose, handwritten papers. Most obvious in the study, though, is the tall Ancient Egyptian sarcophagus that stands in one corner.

### The Desk

**Keeper note:** if Grimm is related to a scout-investigator, access is easier—as they don’t have to sneak in the house; otherwise, make getting to the study _feel_ like a challenge. At minimum, scout-investigators should look inside through the window, before possibly heading off to do research.

The most obvious way in is through the unlocked study door. The other means of ingress would be through a window.

### Inside Grimm’s Study

In the study, the opened wooden crate sits on the floor next to Grimm’s desk. On his desk lies an old,

- The letter is addressed to Colonel Grimm—see **Handout: Box 2** .
- If looking at the journal, ask for a **Library Use** roll. Success finds a page containing instructions for the “Creation of a Magical Powder” (see **Appendix C: Spells** , **Dust of Suleiman** , page 170). Failing that, they can take the journal for later study.
- It could take hours or even days to decipher the scrawled notes and images written on the loose papers;; however, a successful **Spot Hidden** or **Library Use** roll notes a few key words: “ _Package delivery, Seth, Magic Gem, All Seeing._ ” In addition, they find (no roll required) the items noted under **The Loose Papers** (following).


### Chapter 5


- Scout-investigators who possess either the Reading Badge (page 33) or the **Signals and Codes Badge** (page 33) can “check” their badge to understand the symbol is Egyptian, and represents the god Seth.

### The Loose Papers

The pages on the desk are as follows (if desired, Keepers can also include seeds for possible future adventures, see **Scenario Seeds** , page 165.

### The Sarcophagus

Made from wood and ornately decorated with colorful paintwork and gold inlay, the sarcophagus is closed, but not locked. Careful maneuvering enables a scoutinvestigator to open the lid a little.

- A handwritten note— Handout: Box 4 ** —to Grimm referencing progress on something called the “Falstaff Project.” The note is signed by “S.A.“— this refers to Silas Ardmore and the **Treasure of the Secret Way** (page 112) scenario later in this book.

Inside, an Egyptian mummy stands, wrapped in ancient linen; its feet are in a deep pile of dust from centuries of rest.

- A few crumbling pages from an old journal and some handwritten notes, all paper-clipped together. These refer to several accounts from Indigenous peoples and European trappers over hundreds of years. They describe a “vengeful spirit of the forest that brings immortality to those that worship it, and terror to those that anger it,”—this obliquely refers to the events of **Shadow Over Westhaven** (page 132) scenario later in this book. Grimm’s handwritten note reads, _“Can it be hunted?”_

**Keeper note:** while quite dead now, the mummy comes to life later in the scenario.

### The Wicker Box

The box is sealed with twisted copper wires. Opening it requires either a wire-cutting tool—no such tool is handy in the study—and a successful **STR** roll, or someone must take the time to unravel the twisted wires; this task takes at least five minutes (no roll required) but, during this time:

### The Open Wooden Crate

If scout-investigators take time to examine the crate, they find a symbol stamped on it—paper and pencil lie nearby should they wish to make a copy for later research—give players **Handout: Box 3** .

- Ask for a group **Luck** roll:
- **Success:** they are not disturbed and may open the box.
- **Failure:** Grimm comes to the study—see **Grimm Approaches** (following).


### Box of Evil


**Keeper note:** the box is heavy—too heavy for the scout-investigators to carry (it took two men to carry into the house).

### _**grimm Approaches**_

If scout-investigators have a lookout or if one of them succeeds with a **Listen** roll, they hear Grimm‘s approach and have time to hide.

- Ask anyone attempting to hide to make a **Stealth** roll:
- **Success:** Grimm fails to notice them, sits in an armchair, picks up a book, and promptly falls asleep.
- **Failure:** Grimm spots the scout-investigator and asks what they are doing. He tells them off; a convincing excuse combined with **Charm** , **Fast Talk** , or **Persuade** can avoid a call to their parents.


### Homunculus

Approximately 3 feet (0.9 m) tall, it has mottled and leathery skin, vestigial bat-like wings, sharp claws, and a tail. Those getting a close look at the creature see that it seems sewn together from other animal species, like a bizarre chimera. It hisses and bares its razor-sharp teeth at those who approach. For the full profile, see **Homunculus** (page 111).

The homunculus was sent by Thomas Cline to kill Colonel Grimm, rather than the scout-investigators— however, its mission extends to killing everyone in the house; so, any scout-investigators remaining inside become targets after it kills Grimm.

Scout-investigators may, later, discover the homunculus gets noticeably terrified by **The Black Stone** (page 105).

### _**opening the Box**_

When the box is opened, the **Homunculus** (page 111) leaps out. The miniature goblin-like creature hates daylight and immediately dashes for cover, knocking items over and making a racket. It can scale bookcases and furniture with ease and hides well. If anyone attempts to catch it, it tries to bite or claw them. A gap in the baseboard allows escape when the Keeper wishes.

- Ask for a **Cool** roll from everyone in the study.

The homunculus makes noise—which is maybe joined by surprised screams from scout-investigators failing their **Cool** roll. Such noise immediately draws Mrs. Habersham‘s attention as she carries a load of linens to wash. The homunculus hides just as she arrives.

Mrs. Habersham has no patience for the scouts’ outlandish stories about “goblins” or “gremlins,” saying something like, _“Oh, you scamps! You shouldn’t be here. Leave the Colonel’s private belongings be. He is a_
_busy man! Now, go outside and play like normal children before I call the police!”_ She then locks the study and ushers them out of the house.

The homunculus remains hidden throughout and stays quiet, waiting for the scouts to leave.

**Keeper note:** the scout-investigators may not piece it together yet, but if one manages to open the box, ask that character for an **INT** roll (see **Leap of Logic** , page 47): with success, they realize that what Grimm was expecting and what he received are two different objects.

### _**examining the Open Box**_

The wicker box is lined with yellow fabric that appears to be incredibly old and is half filled with fine sand. The cloth has a repeated version of the same symbol as on the outside of the crate—if they have not already received it, give the players **Handout: Box 3** .

### Leaving the Study

If they do not open the box and are not discovered, scout-investigators may have time to copy the symbol on the packing crate and gather the notes or journal to take away. However, if left behind, they remain on the desk the next time the scouts enter the study.

If scout-investigators are discovered, they can’t take anything with them. Still, they can remember the strange symbol and key phrases.

**Keeper note:** if undiscovered, allow scouts enough time to gather clues but don’t give them too long; have Mrs. Habersham arrive to throw them out with a scolding. Additionally, Keepers can remind scout-investigators of their obligations (see **Hobbies** , page 17), which may prompt them to leave.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** to find out more about the symbol on the crate and/or “Falstaff Project” (see **Handout: Box 4** ), use **Research** (nearby).
- **Obvious:** if they released the homunculus, scoutinvestigators may want to research the monster— for all these avenues, see **Research** (neearby).
- **Obvious:** Scout-investigators who copied journal text (see **Inside Grimm’s Study** , page 97) regarding the “Creation of a Magical Powder” may want to create the Dust of Suleiman (see **Create the Dust of Suleiman** , page 103).
- Scout-investigators less interested in research may wish to stick around or go back to Grimm’s mansion to watch or catch the homunculus—see **Return to Grimm’s** , page 103.

### What Happens Next?

The scenario assumes scout-investigators leave or get ejected after entering the study, with dismissal resulting from opening the box and releasing the homunculus. The scouts may then research information found in the study and, possibly, create the “Magical Powder” described in the journal.

Or, perhaps, having seen the homunculus, they decide to study that bizarre creature instead. This is fine, go with the flow; but on releasing the homunculus, the scoutinvestigators should, ideally, be thrown out for a time, to allow events inside the house to gather momentum.

If players aren’t interested in research, think of ways to delay them so that time passes before they return. Consider the scouts’ differing **Obligations** (page 28),  and what parents or guardians say and do if they are called by Grimm or Mrs. Habersham. Perhaps a **Trusted Adult** (page 25) asks them to perform a favor, like helping to carry groceries home, and so on.

However, if players refuse to leave the grounds, then play things out accordingly. They can watch events unfold through the windows and sneak back inside a short time later. As the homunculus ramps up the action, the players can jump in when they see fit. Be flexible and perhaps improvise a little. See **Return to Grimm’s** (page 103) for an overview of how the story should play out; feel free to adapt and change as necessary.

### Research

Several topics come up for scout-investigators to research, depending on events so far. The following section details what can be found in different locations:

- Topic 1:** symbol on the crate—see **Book Research** (following) and/or **At Middlewich College** (page 56).

- Topic 2:**“Falstaff Project”—see**Handout: Box 4 ** and/or **Book Research** (following).

- **Topic 3:** the homunculus—while scout-investigators may not have a name for the creature, they can look up creatures like goblin, gremlin, or little people— see **Book Research** (following).

### Book Research

The same information on the crate’s symbol, the Falstaff Project, and the homunculus can be found in **H. H. Bottoms Library** (page 64) and at **W. Pennyforth Bookseller** (page 61).


### Box of Evil


- Finding relevant clues requires a successful **Library Use** roll for each of the three topics.
- If they fail to find everything in the library, they can try again in the bookshop, or vice versa.
- An Extreme success locates all three topics at once.

### _**topic 1: the Crate’s Symbol**_

- **Success:** the symbol is Ancient Egyptian, and one of them represents the Egyptian god Seth (but does not mention the secret society the Sons of Seth)—give the players **Handout: Box 5** .

### _**topic 2: Falstaff Project**_

- **Success:** finds references to the Falstaff Mining Company, established in 1846 by Heinrich Strauss.
- **Hard success:** give the players **Handout: Treasure 2** (from the next scenario, Treasure of the Secret Way)—a tease for the scenario, although scoutinvestigators cannot act upon it without the map they acquire at the start of that scenario.

### _**topic 3: the Homunculus**_

- **Success:** give the players **Handout: Box 6** .


_Handout: Box 5_ **101**


### At Middlewich College

Professor Bartlett (page 57) in the antiquities department of **Middlewich College** (page 56) has an interest in Egyptology and can provide information about Seth— give players **Handout: Box 5**, if necessary.

- If scout-investigators question her further, ask for a **Charm** , **Fast Talk** , or **Persuade** roll: a success gets the following—anyone with Professor Bartlett as their Trusted Adult automatically succeeds (no roll required).
- **Success:** Doctor Bartlett once wrote a paper on Ancient Egyptian gods. She talks about Egyptian cults and reveals that the Sons of Seth are one such group. Doctor Bartlett provides some general background on the cult’s history—give players **Handout: Box 7.** She shares a persistent rumor that Sons of Seth have a Westhaven temple!

### _**leads**_

- **Obscure:** If scout-investigators want to find out more about the Sons of Seth, they can ask around town. After all, Dr. Bartlett believes a temple exists here—see **Research in Town** (following).

### Research in Town

The scout-investigators could ask around town about the Sons of Seth; sample responses are below. Remember, **Trusted Adults** (page 25) are likely to be more forthcoming; Sons of Seth members feign ignorance of the group’s existence.

- Rusty at **Smith Bros. Salvage** (page 64) **:** _“The Sons of Seth? I had a brother named Seth, but he blew himself up playing with fireworks. He wasn’t too smart, and he certainly didn’t have no sons. Sorry, kids. I can’t help ya.”_
- Bob Harley at **Harley’s Sweet Shoppe** (page 56): _“Well, now. I have been active for a couple of decades in local organizations like the Red Cross and the Masons. My family history goes back several generations. My father and his father belonged to these groups. Our family never belonged to a shadowy group like Sons of Seth. They keep their membership, and everything else, a deep secret. I assumed they were just a social group for wealthy fat cats. I can’t say for sure, but it wouldn’t surprise me if Colonel Grimm belonged. Any fella that keeps so many stuffed heads around his house probably gets into some strange activities.”_

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** some suspect Colonel Grimm belongs to the Sons of Seth.


### Box of Evil

- **Obvious:** having conducted research, the scoutinvestigators can **Return to Grimm’s Study** (page 104) to deal with the homunculus.
- **Obvious:** if they have the text from the journal (see **Inside Grimm’s Study** , page 97) regarding “Creation of a Magical Powder,” they may want to create the Dust of Suleiman (see **Create the Dust of Suleiman** , following).

### Create the Dust of Suleiman

Scout-investigators who copied or took the journal from **Inside Grimm’s Study** (page 97) may want to make some “Magical Powder”—see **Dust of Suleiman** , page 170. A key spell component is dust from a 2,000-yearold mummy—luckily, plenty of dust collected in the bottom of the sarcophagus in Grimm’s study, although scout-investigators don’t know that yet. The other ingredients needed are frankincense, sulfur, and saltpeter.

- **Frankincense:** used in perfumes and soaps. If scoutinvestigators go searching, ask for a **Luck** roll; success means they find it at a private residence of someone who makes their own perfumes, in church (used in incense), or in a laboratory at **Middlewich College** (page 56).
- **Sulfur:** obtainable from the science departments of either **Westhaven Area High School** (page 64) or **Middlewich College** (page 56).
- **Saltpeter:** obtainable from food production plants (curing meat) and local drug stores.

Acquiring these additional ingredients can either be played out or handwaved at the Keeper’s discretion. The task of making the dust takes roughly a day. The powerful dust may prove useful in future adventures, although it can’t help the scout-investigators with the homunculus.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** find out more about the items they found in Grimm’s study—see **Research** , page 100.
- **Obscure:** if the scouts find out about the Sons of Seth, they may wish to research the group—see **Research in Town,** page 102.
- Otherwise, they probably want to **Return to Grimm’s** (following) to watch, catch, or deal with the homunculus.

### Return to Grimm’s

Two possible routes exist: either Colonel Grimm opens the wicker box and releases the homunculus, or the scout-investigators do (see **Opening the Box** , page 99). Time the following events to occur when needed; they can happen soon if the scouts remain on-site watching what’s happening inside, or later after returning from research or chores.

### If Grimm Opens the Box

While the scouts are away, Colonel Grimm returns to his study. If the wicker box is still sealed, he now opens it to find it’s not the treasure he expected. Instead, the room turns pitch black due to a spell cast by the homunculus. While Grimm searches his desk for matches to light the lamp, the creature jumps from the box and opens the sarcophagus. It then casts another spell, causing the mummy to animate.

The pall of darkness suddenly lifts, just in time for Grimm to see the mummy’s desiccated hands reaching for his throat. The colonel grabs his lucky stone from his pocket—but in vain. The homunculus summons a swarm of scarabs. Then, it causes mayhem in the house while trying to eliminate all remaining people inside with the mummy’s help.

**Keeper note:** if the scout-investigators released the homunculus, assume it hides and waits for Grimm in the study. Then, it performs the acts described above.

### The Scouts to the Rescue!

Whether outside or returning to sneak back in, scout-investigators now have the chance to act. The mummy’s attack on Grimm can’t be seen as the drapes are closed, but hopefully, his screams call them to action. If not, have Mrs. Habersham loudly cry out on hearing Grimm’s screams—she goes toward the study to see what’s going on, unless the scouts intercept her (per the skill rolls described below). If the scouts act now, they can “help” Mrs. Habersham get into the study, try to enter the study themselves, or, at least, go see what’s happening.

This time, when the scout-investigators get to the study door, they find it locked:

- If they attempt to pick the lock, call for a **Locksmith** roll: success opens the door.


### Chapter 5

- If they attempt to steal Mrs. Habersham’s house keys, call for a **Sleight of Hand** roll: success gets the keys.
- If they attempt to talk Mrs. Habersham into opening the study door, ask for a **Charm** , **Fast Talk** , or **Persuade** roll (this also works for calming her down and getting the keys if she is in distress outside the property): success opens the door or gets the keys; note if the former, this means she enters the study with the scout-investigators.
- If they attempt to enter through a window, call for a **Stealth** roll: failure knocks something over and the noise brings Mrs. Habersham or the homunculus.

scarabs. Most scurry away, but one scout-investigator (perhaps the one with the lowest Luck or the closest) may be targeted by some of the bugs. They give a sharp bite (but do not cause damage—only if the total mass of beetles attacks a single target is damage taken). The small beetles can be crushed or swatted away in a single round. The scarabs quickly disperse but may come into play later as the scout-investigators move about the house, if desired.

- All scout-investigators should make a **Cool** roll on finding Colonel Grimm dead.
- The scout-investigator who has beetles running up their legs must make a **Cool** roll.

### Return to Grimm’s Study

Inside the study, scouts find the curtains drawn and the lights off, leaving the room dark. If a scout-investigator reaches for the light switch, they discover the horrific sight of Colonel Grimm. He’s sitting at his desk, mouth agape, covered in a writhing black mass of “something.” On the desk in front of him, the wicker box is open.

If the body is disturbed, the wriggling mass falls to the ground and is revealed to be a swarm of flesh-eating

### _**if Mrs. Habersham Enters Alone**_

If the scouts are not with the housekeeper, then her scream as she discovers the body and the beetles is particularly loud; this should bring the scouts into action. They arrive to find her screaming as beetles run around her arms and face before scuttling off to hide.

- Call for a **Cool** roll on seeing the scene in the study.


### Box of Evil

As soon as she’s able, Mrs. Habersham goes to the kitchen to calm down before calling police.

### _**examining the Body**_

Grimm‘s eyes bulge from his sockets, his face frozen in terror. All exposed body parts have bite marks from the scarabs. What’s more, his body has a fine covering of dust. A quick survey reveals that only the sarcophagus has been disturbed; its lid now lies on the floor.

The sarcophagus is empty—if the scout-investigators previously looked inside, they’ll know the mummy has gone; otherwise, they’ll be none the wiser. A pile of dust lies in the bottom of the sarcophagus (use to **Create the Dust of Suleiman** , page 103).

**Keeper note:** now is a perfect time for the weather to sour, with lightning flashing, thunder crashing, and high winds whistling.

- If scout-investigators examine the body, ask for a **First Aid** or **Medicine** roll:
- **Success:** Colonel Grimm has been strangled as his neck shows signs of being crushed. They also spot something in his clenched fist—see **The Black Stone** (following).
- Levering the stone out of Grimm’s dead grip is difficult, requiring a **STR** roll; success means they extract the stone. If they resort to cutting off fingers, whoever wishes to perform the gruesome task must first make a **Cool** roll.

### _**the Black Stone**_

The black stone is the size of an avocado pit. Carved upon it is a Paleolithic-style stick figure that looks like a monkey or lemur. In a spellcaster’s hands, it can be a focus for controlling a homunculus, and the sight strikes terror into any such creature.

**Thomas Cline** (page 60) possesses another such stone and used it when enchanting the homunculus. Unknown to Cline, Grimm also possessed a stone of his own, carried in his pocket. On seeing the creature, Grimm seized his stone, scaring the beast away, but, sadly, the stone was powerless to stop the mummy’s attack.

### Searching the Room

Other than the empty sarcophagus and the various notes and journal (see **Inside Grimm’s Study** , page 97), the only other item of interest is the wicker box.

- No locking mechanism is visible on the wicker box; the wire that had been twisted through the latch held the lid shut.
- The box’s lining consists of an old, yellowed linen fabric; the box is half-filled with fine sand.
- The fabric has the same symbol printed on it as that on the outside of the crate—give players **Handout: Box 3** , if needed.
- If the scout-investigators did not take the notes and journal previously, they can collect and read them now.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** noise comes from the kitchen—proceed to **A Bark & Scream for Help** (following).

### A Bark & Scream for Help

Before the scout-investigators have spent too long in the study, and before Mrs. Habersham has time to call the police, they hear loud barking coming from the kitchen: Grimm’s dog, Buford. Inside the kitchen, the scouts find Buford pacing, sniffing in corners, and barking at something while Mrs. Habersham cries, terrified. Her clothing is torn, and scratches and bites cover her exposed skin.

She sobs, _“Oh, it was terrible! Some vicious little creature attacked me. It came out of nowhere and started biting me. What was that? Ohhhhh!”_ Mrs. Habersham is too frightened to leave the kitchen. Buford seems deeply concerned and refuses to leave her.

- Scouts can provide **First Aid** (page 41) for Mrs. Habersham before leaving, restoring 1D4 hit points.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** crashing sounds from upstairs **(Upstairs Havoc** , below).
- **Obvious:** the mummy bumping around **(Downstairs Desperation** , page 106).
- Do they split up?

### Upstairs Havoc

A loud crash emanates from upstairs, like glass and pottery breaking. This is followed by another crash and then, a loud thump. If scouts investigate, they hear a crash coming from the **Main Bedroom** as they reach the top of the stairs.

The door to the main bedroom is ajar. If a scout approaches it, one of Grimm’s expensive vases comes flying at them.


- Ask the scout concerned for a **Dodge** roll:
- **Failure:** they suffer 1D3 damage as the vase shatters against their head.

Keeper note: currently, the homunculus is down to 5 magic points; that’s enough to use Create Darkness (see **Homunculus**, page 111) one more time to escape the scouts. After this, it can’t cast any more spells.

Upon entering, the scouts see the homunculus grinning evilly at them. Then, suddenly, the room fills with inky darkness—whereupon the homunculus flees the room, down the hall, and back downstairs.

- If a scout-investigator holds The Black Stone (page 105), the homunculus is noticeably disturbed by it; those present should make an **INT** roll (see **Leap of Logic**, page 47) to figure that the homunculus is somehow frightened of the stone. Perhaps the stone could drive the beast back into its box?

### Downstairs Desperation

The homunculus ordered the mummy to ambush Mrs. Habersham, and it has been slowly searching for her. The mummy now finds Mrs. Habersham and attacks. This scene should happen soon after **A Bark & Scream for Help** (page 105) or a little later, after the scouts go upstairs.

- If the scout-investigators went up to investigate, they come down to find the mummy strangling Mrs. Habersham in the kitchen. Buford is growling and pulling on the mummy’s wrappings. If the scouts can intervene, they can save the housekeeper; otherwise, her limp, lifeless body falls to the floor with a dull thud. The hollow-eyed horror then turns to the scout-investigators.
- Alternatively, if any of the scouts remained in the kitchen with Mrs. Habersham, they are present when the mummy enters and attacks. It ignores the scoutinvestigators at first, attempting to strangle the terrified housekeeper—it succeeds unless the scouts somehow prevent it. Once this task has been completed, the mummy pursues the scout-investigators. For the full profile, see **Mummy** , page 111.


> — BOX OF EVIL

- Witnessing the mummy and Mrs. Habersham’s lifeless body triggers a **Cool** roll.

Keeper note: the optional Luck Spending rule (page 41) gives players the chance to adjust poor skill rolls. Should events lead to the worst, the **Cheating Death** rule (page 42) can save their scout-investigator from certain death.

### Playing Out the Struggle

The struggle with the homunculus and mummy can play out in a variety of ways. Several options are considered here.

### _**back in Your Box!**_

- If scout-investigators attempt to herd the homunculus back into the wicker box, treat this as an **Assisted Fighting Maneuver** (page 38).
- If a scout is using **The Black Stone** (page 105), grant the group one bonus die for the maneuver.
- If the scout-investigators succeed in forcing the homunculus back into the wicker box, they must re-seal the box somehow—such as with twisted wire— while one works to keep it closed (e.g., sitting on top).

### _**trap an Enemy in a Room**_

- If a scout-investigator wishes to lock or bar a door, ask for a **Luck** roll:
- **Success:** if they are inside the room, they can bolt the door. Or, if they possess Mrs. Habersham’s keys, they can lock the door.
- **Failure:** the door has no lock or bolt.

### Breaking Through a Door

- Breaking through a door requires a Hard **STR** roll. Or a lock may be picked with **Locksmith** or Hard **Mechanical Repair** .
- If the mummy attempts to break through a door, ask for a group **Luck** roll each round; failure means the door splinters and crashes open.

### _**mummy Issues**_

The mummy moves slowly. It shuffles along with a stiff, halting gait; however, it does not tire and pursues the scouts with the intent of completing its orders of killing everyone in the house.

This may set up additional encounters as the mummy follows the scouts, while the homunculus tries to trip or bite them, throws objects, and so on. Scout-investigators may even try to set traps to capture or immobilize


their foes—reward ingenuity and decide which skill or characteristic roll can determine if their plan works. If they coordinate and enact the plan with skilled teamwork, consider awarding one bonus die.

### _**it’s a Wrap!**_

Scout-investigators may attempt to bind the mummy up in a bedsheet or similar.

- Treat this as an **Assisted Fighting Maneuver** (page 38). With success, the mummy is bound and unable to attack.
- Ask for a group **Luck** roll at the end of each subsequent round **:** failure sees the mummy tear its way free of the sheet.

### _**push Mummy Down the Stairs!**_

The mummy pursues the scout-investigators through the house. If upstairs, the scout-investigators can attempt to lure it to the top and push it down.

- Treat this as an **Assisted Fighting Maneuver** (page 38) and award one bonus die if the mummy is bound in a sheet already (see **It’s a Wrap** , preceding).
- Tumbling down the stairs causes the mummy 1D8 damage (the damage ignores armor) and renders it inactive for the following round.


### _**flames!**_

Scout-investigators may use the **Immolate** spell given to them by Boxcar Jim (see **Boxcar Jim’s Useful Items** , page 63). Or they could try to ignite it by a more traditional method—but that likely involves getting in range of its deadly grasp.

If the mummy is killed with fire, unless scoutinvestigators act immediately to extinguish the flames, they quickly spread to the soft furnishings; soon, the whole mansion is ablaze, ultimately burning to the ground. Almost all evidence gets destroyed by the fire; the only clues left are the charred remains of Colonel Grimm, Mrs. Habersham (if she didn’t escape), and the mummy.

### Fleeing the Mansion

When scout-investigators exit the mansion, they are surprised to discover the building has been transported into an endless desert. The sun beats down upon them with an intense, dry heat. Nothing but sand in any direction, except for one striking object. About 1 mile (1.6 km) away stands a large pyramid close to completion. One block remains to install, and workers are preparing to place it now.

**Keeper note:** the desert scene is an illusion, a magical hallucination created when the mummy was animated. Once the scouts exit the mansion, they become subject to the illusion.

- Using fire against the homunculus requires a successful **DEX** roll. The fire causes 1D6 points of damage, but it only burns for one round, then the flames die.
- Against the mummy, the flames automatically take hold (no roll). The fire causes 1D6 damage per round (ignore armor) until it runs out of hit points.
- If desired, the homunculus could try to extinguish the burning mummy by throwing a vase of water. The scouts can try to stop this with a successful **DEX** roll to catch it in midair. If they fail, call for a group **Luck** roll; failure extinguishes the fire!
- Witnessing this strange, out-of-place scene demands a **Cool** roll.
- A successful **INT** roll (see **Leap of Logic** , page 47) reveals they cannot return to their world while the mummy survives.

### Ending the Illusion

When no living humans remain in the house, the mummy re-enters its sarcophagus and returns to its former state. At this point, the illusion disappears, everything reverts to normal. Otherwise, the illusion
stops when the mummy is destroyed or if it has been trapped back inside the sarcophagus.

have not already done so. This is about the time police turn up.

### The Police Arrive

### Wrapping Things Up

The scenario ends when the mummy is either destroyed, disenchanted, or trapped in its sarcophagus. In most cases, this happens when either the scoutinvestigators defeat it or Cline turns up to save the day (see **Cline to the Rescue** , following).

### Cline to the Rescue

If the fight goes poorly for the scout-investigators, have Cline arrive to save the day. He strides in as if he owns the place, finds the mummy, and then speaks arcane words that disenchant it and end the desert illusion (see **Fleeing the Mansion** , page 108). He also possesses a **Black Stone** (page 105) and uses it to force the homunculus back into the wicker box, if scouts After the mummy is defeated, the scout-investigators hear banging on front door. Then, police officers enter, led by **Andy Coolidge** (page 59). They usher the scouts and anyone else still alive from the building. If necessary, **First Aid** (see **Wounds & Healing** , page 41) is provided before they send the scouts home. Those protesting are picked up and put in the back of a police car to await their parents or guardians—who look quite unhappy.

Police and other adults dismiss any accounts of what happened as the _“overactive imagination of youth”_ or _“trauma-induced hallucinations.”_ The police insist that what the scouts claimed was a mummy was just a burglar. _“It was probably Boxcar Jim! I’ve seen him skulking around town again. Go pick him up.”_ The homunculus is dismissed as _“an escaped zoo animal,”_ or, _“a squirrel with rabies.”_ As for the scarabs, officers say, _“That’s what you get for illegally importing dangerous artifacts.”_


### Chapter 5

If any of the scout-investigators are members of the Junior Police Corps or Junior Firefighter, an officer or firefighter may recognize them. If this person is the scout-investigator’s Trusted Adult, this can lead to some interesting discussions, and they likely give the youngster’s story more credence.

If scouts provide physical evidence, it mysteriously disappears from the police evidence locker after wealthy, powerful Sons of Seth contacts reach out.
