### _**special Abilities**_

Create Darkness (cost 5 MP):** creates a sphere of darkness, 6 feet (1. 8 m) in diameter, centered on the homunculus and moves with it. All light in the area is drained away, blinding everyone within. The effect lasts three minutes.

After using its various magical powers to kill Colonel Grimm, the homunculus has 5 magic points remaining; enough to use Create Darkness one more time around the scout-investigators. Scouts in darkness suffer one penalty die to attack rolls.

**Animate Mummy & Summon Scarab Beetles:** these two powers are only used once in this scenario.


Mummy, _**shambling horror**_ STR 105 CON 80 SIZ 65 DEX 35 INT 50 APP— POW 75 EDU— SAN— HP 14 DB: +1D6 Build: 2 Move: 4 MP: 15 Luck:—

### _**combat**_

**Attacks per round:** 1 (kick, strike, etc.)

### _**special Abilities**_

**Strangle (mnvr):** both hands grab a target’s throat. On the following round, it deals 2D6 damage per round until the victim is dead, incapacitated, or the mummy is thrown off. The victim may break free with an opposed STR roll. Should others inflict 6 or more damage to the mummy in one round, it releases its vice-like hold.

### _**skills**_

Jump 65%, Listen 50%, Stealth 75%.

**Armor:** none, but attack rolls to hit a running homunculus are made with one penalty die.

Fighting 70% (35/14), damage 1D6+1D6 Dodge 17% (8/3)

### _**skills**_

Stealth 50%, Unearthly Moan 90%.

### **spells:** None.

**Cool:** make a **Cool** roll the first time encountering a homunculus leaping about.

**Armor:** 2-point tough skin; impaling weapons deal half damage, while firearms deal 1 damage maximum.

**Spells:** none.

**Cool:** make a **Cool** roll on encountering a mummy shuffling around.


### Chapter 6: Treasure of the Secret Way

This scenario is playable by up to six scoutinvestigators and takes approximately T one or two sessions to complete. Here, scout-investigators receive a crudely drawn map to a lost gold mine, allowing them to do some treasure hunting. Yet, they aren’t the only ones to have stumbled onto the mine’s location. Conman Silas Ardmore has the same map; he previously approached retired adventurer **Josef Grimm** (see **Box of Evil** , page 88) about helping to bankroll an expedition in return for a cut.

### Keeper Information

In 1846, just before the California gold rush, a German coal miner named Heinrich Strauss emigrated to the US dreaming of a new life. Strauss settled in Westhaven and began to survey the region for mining opportunities. After months of studying geological maps and hiking through the foothills and ranges, Strauss came across a range of rocky hills northwest of town that seemed perfect. He dug a series of test pits and channels, and it didn’t take long to recover a few ounces (100 g) of gold dust. He was going to be rich!

Returning to Westhaven, Strauss formed the Falstaff Mining Company (he was a Shakespeare fan) and began recruiting a team. His mining crew consisted of himself and two other men. Every few weeks, they visited Westhaven for supplies. Strauss took care that no one followed them for fear of claim jumpers. Despite some initial success, as summer turned to fall, tempers began to fray.

Money was running out and everyone was growing frustrated. The small flakes of gold were failing to turn into a bigger find.

Westhaven residents noted Strauss and his crew had stopped coming into town altogether. No one ever saw them again. Rumors circulated that Strauss secretly struck it big and retired in luxury, while other stories had more sinister endings. Eventually, the legend of the “lost prospector” became a myth that has now been retold thousands of times. It eventually became known as the “Treasure of the Secret Way.”

The real story is simple: Strauss fell out with his workers, and a fight broke out. Strauss died alone in the mine, jealously guarding his find. Now, his ghost roams the mine.

Presently, conman Silas Ardmore is secretly working the mine; the last thing he wants is meddling scouts getting in his way. So, not only must the scouts contend with Ardmore, but also with a restless spirit!

### Involving the Scout-investigators

Boxcar Jim presents the scout-investigators with a map that, with a little work on their part, leads them to Strauss’ gold mine. Those who played **Box of Evil** (page 88) may have learned of the **Legend of the Treasure of the Secret Way** (see **Handout: Treasure 2** ); however, lacking the map, this provided only a teaser for this scenario. Now, they’ve remembered that old legend, and being two years older, they feel ready to go exploring for gold.


### Chapter 6

- **Playing as a stand-alone scenario:** any scoutinvestigators can be used.
- **Playing as part three of the Westhaven Campaign:** two years have passed since **Box of Evil** (page 88), and it is now 1922 (the exact date is unimportant); scoutinvestigators should now be **Rangers** (page 29).
- **Traits:** rubs his stubbled chin while listening, as if unsure of everything said to him.
- **Roleplaying Hooks:** Ardmore is trying to discover gold after finding a map to the old mine. When he discovers the scouts are also hunting for the gold, he attempts to scare them away by posing as the ghost.

### Dramatis Personae


### **silas Ardmore,** _**age 34**_ **,** _**conman**_

Ardmore recently finished a two-year sentence for burglary. Armed with a map to Strauss’ old mine, he located and began working the mine, but, so far, with little success. He saw **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** (page 131), but because he’s found no gold, he has not drawn its ire. What’s more, Ardmore wears a talisman of protection (see **Baba Itzak’s Brooch** , following) given to him by Colonel Grimm. So far, he believes it to be working, as the ghost has left him alone.


### Baba Itzak’s Brooch

This old pewter brooch bearing a curious design protects the wearer from psychic attacks—such as those made by Strauss’ ghost. The brooch grants the wearer one bonus die on opposed POW rolls made versus psychic attack, as well as spell attacks.

The brooch is currently in Silas Ardmore‘s possession, but, at a point of the Keeper’s choosing, he loses it—and fails to notice its absence until targeted by the ghost.

Ardmore‘s camp is located over a hill, away from the mine’s main entrance.

- **Description:** hunched, yet average build, with greasy black hair and a short beard; always furtive.


> — TREASURE OF THE SECRET WAY

### Leads: Obscure & Obvious Clues

At the end of key sections, we list “leads” to help Keepers run scenarios smoothly. This provides clear routes for scout-investigators to follow in terms of clues and plot progression. The leads tell Keepers which scene or event comes next, navigating the path the story can take from here.

- If the lead is labeled “obvious”—make scoutinvestigators aware of it as the scene progresses. They usually do not require a skill roll. Obvious leads are essential to the plot.
- If the lead is labeled “obscure”—the clue usually requires some form of specific action or a skill roll to access the information; or it may be available from a more easily accessible alternative source. Usually, obscure clues are not necessary to move the scenario forward.

### Start: Picnic in Central Park

The scout-investigators are hanging out in Westhaven Central Park (page 58), working on an orienteering task that **Paul** (page 56), the scout leader, set up earlier. As the scouts are using their map and compass to find each of the waypoints of the course, they hear some rustling from a nearby clump of trees. Hiding in the bushes, they see **Boxcar Jim** (page 62), a transient and friend to the scouts. His familiar voice speaks softly:

> _“Psst! Hey, how are my favorite Daniel Boones? Did you miss your old pal, Jim? I know I haven’t been around for a while, but I was over in Arkham for a spell. I had a line on some decent food getting thrown out behind a restaurant each night. I was eating like a king. Well, until they chased me off. What are ya going to do? But while there, I came across something amazing, and I thought of you._

**115** **CHAPTER 6**


### Treasure of the Secret Way

> _“It was like that time in Casablanca when I accidentally walked into an illegal gambling den in the back of a night club. Earlier, I’d been blessed by an old woman reading tea leaves. She said I was going to have a profitable day. At one point, I was up 2,000 francs! But then, the owner started casting an evil eye at me. I mean, literally. His eyes turned red, and my head began swimming. I started getting woozy, so I cashed out and went to a smoking den. I was there for a week… I think. I don’t know, I lost track of time. Wait… what was I talking about?_

> _“Oh yeah! I was walking through a used bookstore coz’ it was raining. Anyway, I was reading an old book on local legends written by a Miskatonic University professor; suddenly, a folded-up piece of paper fell out onto the floor. Sure enough, when I picked the paper up, it had an honestto-goodness treasure map on it. I couldn’t believe it!_

> _“Well, I immediately thought of you kids. With your skills and attitude, I am sure you could find it and make some real money. What would you all spend it on? I would do it myself, but my back’s been acting up. I am happy to give you the map, but would you be able to give old Jim a few quarters?”_

Boxcar Jim then hands the treasure map to the scouts—give players **Handout: Treasure 1** . But, before they can really study it, Scout Leader Glenn comes over to tell them to go finish the orienteering course. He then sees Boxcar Jim and admonishes him for talking with the youths, telling him to get lost.

However, _Campfire Tales_ tries to capture the feel of being scouts, and being prepared is important to a scout—especially when preparing for an expedition.

You can, of course, handwave the equipping stage if you wish, or ask players what they are taking and have them note it down. Don’t track money and costs if the scouts only take mundane items like those on the **Recommended Provisions** (following).

If you handwave making lists, but later need to determine if a scout-investigator brought a particular item:

- Ask for a **Luck** roll:
- **Success:** they packed that piece of equipment.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** to make best use of the map, some **Research** (below) is required.

### Recommended Provisions

- Bedroll
- Buckets
- Canteen with water
- Compass
- Food for four days (apples, bread, jerky, oatmeal, etc.)
- Lanterns and oil
- Matches

### Preparing for the Hunt

Equipped with the treasure map (see Handout: Treasure 1 **), the scouts need a plan if they wish to find the legendary gold. This involves inventing a suitable cover story so as not to alarm adults about their actual goal—or attract those wishing to steal the gold. Saying the group is camping, as Wayfarer Scouts often do, should suffice. If desired, include some roleplaying with adults, especially if their absence conflicts with their obligations (see **Hobbies**, page 17).

Before embarking, scout-investigators must acquire the necessary provisions. Scouts should consider this carefully, as they can only bring whatever they can comfortably carry on their backs. **Bailey’s Top-Quality Hardware** (page 58) sells any gear they do not currently have. Other options include sneaking gear from their families.

**Keeper note:** when playing _Call of Cthulhu_ , the focus is roleplay and adventure, and less attention is usually paid to acquisition of equipment and provisions.

- Mess kit (bowl, fork, knife, plate)
- Knapsack
- Picks

- Rope (50 feet/ 15 m)

- Shovels
- Spare clothes

### Research

- To check their map against maps at **H. H. Bottoms Library** (page 64), ask for a **Library Use** roll.
- **Success:** they figure the mine is in a hilly region known as the Olde Green Forest, requiring a 14 mile (23 km) hike northeast out of town; they also work out the optimal route—earning them one bonus die when later making a Navigation roll on their way to the mine.
- **Failure:** they figure the mine’s approximate location in the Olde Green Forest, requiring a 14 mile (23 km) hike northeast out of town.


### Chapter 6

- To research the history of gold mining in the Westhaven area, ask for another **Library Use** roll.
- **Success:** give **Handout: Treasure 2** to the players.
- **Failure:** they find little, just headlines of an old legend about a “lost prospector” and his hidden gold mine.

Keeper note: players may have previously acquired Handout: Treasure 2 **when following up on a letter found in Grimm’s Study (if they played **Box of Evil**, see page 88). Astute scout-investigators may recall that the letter (see**Handout: Box 4 **) mentioned a “brooch”  (see **Baba Itzak’s Brooch**, page 114) given to Ardmore by Grimm; this highlights its importance to scout-investigators.

### Boxcar Jim Helps Out

As the scouts prepare, Boxcar Jim runs up, looking even wilder and more frantic than usual. Unusually, he seems afraid. He says:

> _“Oh, thank goodness! You haven’t left yet! I was worried I wouldn’t see you before you go. My usual nightmares were much, much worse. I’m extremely worried for your little adventure. I hope those old ghost stories aren’t true. Here, please take this. Hopefully, it will help protect you.”_

Jim hands the scouts a small, glass bottle with a cork stopper. Inside, a piece of rolled-up parchment has **Banish the Visitor** written on it (see **Boxcar Jim’s Useful Items** , page 63).

- If scout-investigators ask around town, one of their Trusted Adults (page 25) may link the mining and the initials “S.A.” noting that SA could mean **Silas Ardmore** (page 114). Ardmore has an evil reputation and is wanted for questioning by police officer **Andy Coolidge** (page 59). Ardmore was let out of jail and, since then, he’s gone missing—the police want to talk to him about some recent thefts.

### Hiking to the Mine

The hike takes the scouts about one day. This presumes a lunch break and a quick swim in a pond or other activity.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** the scout-investigators now know the mine’s location and can begin **Hiking to the Mine** (nearby)—but not before they meet Boxcar Jim again (see **Boxcar Jim Helps Out** , following).
- Ask the scout-investigator reading the map for a **Navigate** roll (maybe the one with the highest Navigate) to follow landmarks on the crudely drawn map; grant one bonus die if their **Research** (page 117) was successful:


- **Success:** they make excellent time, **Arriving Before Dark** (following).
- **Failure:** they become lost, **Arriving After Dark** (below).

As they cross the small clearing to reach the base of the hill, the wolves start to clear the tree line. Time is of the essence!

- Ask for a **Natural World** roll:

### Arriving Before Dark

Following the map leads the scout-investigators to **The Mine Entrance** (page 120). They arrive late afternoon, safely before dark. Getting to the entrance is a climb, but they are not in a hurry, so no skill roll is required.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** proceed to **The Mine Entrance** (page 120).

### Arriving After Dark

Around 30 minutes or so before sunset, the scouts hear howling. Just as the sun sets and dusk falls, the scouts spy an opening halfway up a rocky hill. It looks like an old mine entrance, but requires a steep climb to get there. The sound of hungry wolves draws ever closer. As darkness arrives, worrying shapes move through the trees toward the scouts.

- **Success:** a pack of wolves is closing on them, and they are on the hunt!
- Seeing a wolf pack racing toward them triggers a **Cool** roll.
- Ask for a **Climb** roll to scramble up the rocky hill and reach the mine’s entrance—those who drop their pack now can reach the mine entrance without a roll (automatic success), otherwise:
- **Success:** the scout makes it safely to **The Mine Entrance** (page 120).
- **Failure:** they slip and are struggling—the wolves are closing in! Now, they have a choice: jettison their pack and automatically reach the entrance or keep their pack and push the roll. Make it clear that if they fail, the wolves get them.
- **Fumble or a failed pushed roll:** the scout-investigator must fight off a **Wolf Attack** (following).


### Chapter 6

Once all scouts successfully enter the mine, the wolves linger for 30 minutes before moving on. The wolves’ howls echo through the forest for hours.

### The Mine Entrance

**Keeper note:** the crude map is not an accurate representation of the mine’s layout. This may lead to some confusion among scout-investigators.

### Wolf Attack

Wolf attacks are rare, as the animals are usually wary of adult humans. But the scouts aren’t adults yet, so the wolves attack any target failing to reach the safety of the mine’s entrance.

Rather than use full combat rules here, when a scout-investigator is attacked, ask if they are dodging or fighting back.

- Depending on the response, ask for either a **Dodge** or **Fighting (Brawl)** roll:
- **Success:** they either avoid the wolf or manage to hit it—either way, they now reach **The Mine Entrance** (following).
- **Failure:** the wolf bites into the scout’s leg, tearing their clothing and causing 1D6 damage. The scout’s screams and flailing limbs scare the wolf into backing away; this allows the scout-investigator to reach **The Mine Entrance** (following).
- Optionally, call for a **Cool** roll for scouts who have a close encounter with a wolf.

The opening looks more like a cave than a proper mine entrance, being just 3 feet (1 m) round. Inside, rotting hand-hewn support timbers provide structure to the opening, which extends another 30 feet (9 m) before ending in darkness. As the scouts catch their breath, stale air emanates from the passage ahead, unsettling to the stomach.

Scouts with light sources (flashlight, etc.) can crawl down to the end of the 30 foot (9 m) entrance tunnel; the passage opens out to **Area A** (following). If everyone jettisoned their packs to evade the wolves, allow the scout-investigator with the highest Luck to have a light source (no roll required)—ask the player concerned what they have. Perhaps some matches in their jacket pocket, a lighter, or a small flashlight?

### Area A: Staging Area

This larger area is approximately 30 feet (9 m) in diameter with a ceiling height of 8 feet (2. 5 m) and appears to be the main staging area for the old mine. The air is dry and dusty. Some rusted tools lay discarded on the floor: a metal shovel head (completely


**CHAPTER 6** rusted through and unusable) and a heavy iron pick with a broken handle.

The cavern is spacious and would make a decent campsite. At least it gets the scouts out of the elements and keeps them safe from the wolves! The scouts may elect to make their camp here, setting up their gear and preparing dinner (if they still have their packs) or they can explore.

### _**making Camp**_

- Ask for a **Survival** roll when setting up camp:
- **Success:** they figure that a cooking fire could be built out by the mine entrance, on the flat, well-ventilated area just outside.
- **Failure:** the scout-investigators build their fire inside the cave, too far from the entrance; the area fills with smoke, making it difficult to breath. This can be resolved without further dice rolls.

**Keeper note:** if players state they are building a fire outside _before_ the Survival roll is made, assume failure means they have trouble lighting it. They can push the roll; a second failure means they either exhaust their fire-making materials, or the scout concerned burns themself, causing 1D2 damage.

### _**let’s Explore**_

Three shafts branch off **Area A** ; each has wooden beams supporting the roof at approximately 10 foot (3 m) increments.

- Call for a **Spot Hidden** or **Track** roll:
- **Success:** the scout-investigator notices prints in the dirt. Most are made by animals (mice, raccoons, rats, and even some bird tracks); however, some boot tracks go off down each shaft, but their age is unclear.

Exploring short lengths of each passage, they discover:

- The northern and eastern passages are both blocked by cave-ins at **Area C** (page 123) and **Area B** (page 123), respectively. They occur approximately 20–30 feet (6–9 m) in.

- The western passage travels for 50 feet (15 m) before turning north.

If the scouts investigate the cave-ins Area C (page 123) and **Area B** (page 123) further:

- **Success:** they determine that the northern passage’s cave-in is traversable with a little effort; however, the eastern passage’s cave-in requires significant work and time to excavate enough debris to squeeze through.

### Spooky Events

**Silas Ardmore** (page 114) becomes aware of the meddling scouts and seeks to frighten them away with cheap scares. He always maintains a safe distance and plans his escape route carefully to avoid detection. But Ardmore’s fakery is not the only source of spookiness, as the real ghost of Heinrich Strauss haunts the area. Use the following events, at the Keeper’s discretion, as the group explores the old mine:

- Ardmore creates a low moan or chilling scream emanating from deep within the dark mine. It is deeply unsettling—ask for a **Cool** roll.
- A strong gust of wind rushes through the passage, strangely coming from deeper inside the mine. Any lanterns, candles, or open fires are snuffed out— ask for a **Cool** roll. Note: this natural phenomenon occurs as air is drawn through the mine’s rear (secret) entrance.
- A mournful moan comes from down the passage. As the scouts turn to the sound, they catch a glimpse of a ghostly figure, which appears and then disappears quickly—ask for a **Cool** roll. Note: the Keeper decides whether this is Ardmore or the real ghost.
- The sound of metal scraping on stone nearby—ask for a **Cool** roll. Note: this is Strauss’ ghost dragging its phantom pickaxe along the tunnels’ floor.
- If scouts hurry toward the strange lights or sounds, ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll:
- **Success:** if Ardmore caused the disturbance, the scouts catch sight of him hurrying away. Do they pursue him? But if the real ghost caused it, they come face-to-face with **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** (page 128), which screams _“Be gone!”_ before disappearing—triggering a **Cool** roll.
- **Failure:** whatever it was leaves before the scouts get there. If it was Ardmore, they find adult-sized boot prints—do ghosts wear boots? If the scouts attempt to follow the boot prints, ask for a **Track** roll: with success, depending on the “haunting’s” location, they may either find Ardmore or his **Secret Entrance** (page 125).
- Ask for a **Geology** roll:


> — TREASURE OF THE SECRET WAY


### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** the spooky events may lead to an encounter either with **Silas Ardmore** (page 114) or **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** (page 128).
- **Obscure:** following Ardmore’s boot prints leads to the **Secret Entrance** (page 125).

### Area B: East Passage

The eastern passage out of **Area A** (page 120) is blocked by a cave-in. It requires several hours labor to move the debris to make it passable.

- Ask for a **Geology** roll to safely clear a way through:
- **Success:** using old timbers lying around, they build structural bracing and prevent a cave-in.
- **Failure:** the unstable ceiling and walls collapse. Affected scouts should make a **Dodge** roll to avoid suffering 1D6 damage from falling rocks.

**Keeper note:** Ardmore has not yet explored beyond this point.

### Area C: Blocked Passage

The northern passage out of **Area A** (page 120) is partially blocked by a cave-in. A narrow opening allows only one person at a time to squeeze through. As the scouts crawl through, smaller rocks fall from the ceiling onto them—no dice rolls are required, but this should give them a clue that the ceiling is unstable.

- Scraps of half-buried rotting wood all have a covering of dust.
- Ask for a **Geology** roll: success confirms the passage is not in danger of imminent collapse.

If scouts search the area:

### Area D: the Ossuary

- As the scout-investigators walk down the passageway toward the Ossuary, ask for a **Geology** roll: success realizes the passage is descending deeper underground.

The passage opens into a room, 20 by 30 feet (6 by 9 m). This area seems colder than the others (due to cold air settling at this lower point in the mine). A quick search reveals a few unsettling items:

- Ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll—if they say they are pulling up some of the half-buried wood, they don’t need to roll and find the following automatically.
- **Success:** a skeletal hand buried in the dusty ground, which connects to a full skeleton. Ask for a **Cool** roll.

**Keeper note:** the skeleton is that of one of Heinrich Strauss’ mining crew.

- The walls show signs of numerous test holes. Most are only a foot or two (half a meter) in diameter and are anywhere from 10 inches (25 cm) to 1 yard/ meter deep.
- Anyone inspecting the holes should make an **INT** roll (see **Leap of Logic** , page 47): success means they figure the holes are recent due to lack of oxidization of freshly scrapped iron.
- Scouts who investigate the skeleton easily discern it has been here a long time (no roll).
- A successful **First Aid** , **Medicine** or **Natural World** roll suggest this person died a violent death. The skeleton has a crushed skull and deep cut marks on the ribcage indicating he was stabbed to death.


### Chapter 6

### Area E: the Hall of Voices

Keeper note: the northern passage from Area A (page 120) is partially blocked by a cave-in (see **Area C**, page 123).

full damage—depending on the situation, Keepers may allow a **Jump** or **Dodge** roll to avoid the worst, reducing the damage to 1D10 damage.

The Hall of Voices is 30 foot (9 m) square and has a domed ceiling. This area possesses bizarre acoustic properties; the sounds of the scouts’ footsteps and voices roll around the walls before being carried off through the mine.

The exits to the east and west are impassable. The scouts may spend several tiring hours clearing either cave-in, but eventually, they realize these are abandoned tunnels that lead nowhere.

- Call for an **INT** roll (see **Leap of Logic** , page 47) either now or later: success means they realize the link between the strange acoustics in this area and the demoniacal moaning they’ve heard.

### Area F: the Doom

This square area is approximately 30 feet (9 m) on each side and has a low ceiling, 3 feet (1 m) high. On entering, it smells awful, and everything is covered with dust. The place is cluttered with old boxes, barrels, rusted equipment, and decaying wood. Several boxes and barrels are still sealed. Numerous dead birds, rats, and bats litter the floor.

Natural methane (tainted with sulfur which gives it the rotten smell) is leaking into this room from cracks in the ground; the gas then gets pulled out through a fissure along the north wall, containing it to this room rather than filling the entire mine.

If scouts search through the boxes and barrels, they find most of the contents have either rotted away (e.g., grain, meal, rope) or have rusted beyond use. In addition, they can find:

- An iron pry bar, which can be swung like a club (1D8+DB damage; but see **Cumbersome Weapons** , page 40).
- One box containing six sticks of dynamite (4D10 damage to anyone within 3 yards/meters). The chemicals in the dynamite have become unstable, and the sticks are weeping explosive chemicals. A scout with the **Young Scientist** hobby automatically knows the dynamite is unsafe; otherwise, a **Know** (EDU) roll may confirm the same.
- Scouts collecting the dynamite should be extremely careful! If a stick is thrown or dropped, it may explode—call for a **Luck** roll to determine if the dynamite explodes. A failure means the scout suffers

While the methane gas remains below the level at which it is explosive:

- Those who spend more than two minutes exploring this area begin to feel lightheaded and must make a **CON** roll:
- **Success:** they feel light-headed; if they stay in this area, they must make another **CON** roll every two minutes.
- **Failure:** they start to collapse and will fall unconscious unless they get out or are taken out of Area F. If alone, they must make another CON roll with one penalty die to crawl out before they fall unconscious. If needed, a successful First Aid roll can help revive anyone unconscious; however, simply getting the scout to fresh air and allowing them to rest means they recover without a roll.

Keeper note: the optional Luck Spending rule (page 41) gives players the chance to adjust poor skill rolls; should events lead to the worst, the **Cheating Death** rule (page 42) can save the scout-investigators from certain death.

### Area G: the Grand Ballroom

This large area measures 90 by 50 feet (27 by 15m) with a 12 foot (4 m) irregular ceiling. It looks big enough to host a ball. A crack in the center of the ceiling extends all the way to the surface; the crack acts like a chandelier, allowing sunlight to pierce the area during the day, while pale rays of moonlight beam down at night.

Numerous holes dug along the walls give them the appearance of Swiss cheese. If examined, the holes appear recent, due to fresh tool marks. If scouts investigate the holes or the room, a huge swarm of bats emerges from the many holes, quickly filling the room before flying out toward the entrance.

- Ask for a **Cool** roll as bats fly around the investigators’ heads.

This area contains Silas Ardmore’s **Secret Entrance** (following) to the mine. It’s extremely difficult to discover, as it’s near the ceiling, and the shadows created by flashlights or lanterns make it almost impossible to see. The scouts might be alerted to a potential exit here if they see Silas Ardmore enter and then apparently disappear.


> — TREASURE OF THE SECRET WAY

- If so, ask for a Hard **Spot Hidden** roll: success spots the **Secret Entrance** (following).

A short path through the woods leads to **Ardmore’s Campsite** (following), his home for the past few weeks.

### _**leads**_

- **Obscure:** scout-investigators finding the **Secret Entrance** (following) may also learn of **Ardmore’s Campsite** (nearby).

### Secret Entrance

Reaching the exit from the floor requires a climb. Scouts can use the excavated test holes as hand- and footholds to reach a hidden ledge; from here, a small shaft leads upward.

### Ardmore’s Campsite

Should scout-investigators find the campsite, most likely after following the secret entrance in **Area G** (page 124), they discover a small canvas tent, a firepit, some clothes hanging on a piece of rope, several tools (pickaxe, shovel, etc.), and some food (tinned peaches, jerky, etc.). Depending on the time of day, Silas Ardmore may be present (see **Meeting Ardmore** , page 127).

### Area H: the Pit

- Ask for a **Climb** roll—if using rope and/or working together, grant one bonus die:
- **Success:** they reach the secret shaft that leads outside.
- **Failure:** make a **Cool** roll to see if they hold their nerve; they may push this roll.
- **Fumble or failed pushed roll:** the scout either becomes stranded or falls, suffering 1D6 damage.

The narrow shaft is tight for an adult, but the scouts find it easy to crawl through. The shaft runs nearly 100 feet (30 m) and ends on the other side of the rocky hill.

- Ask anyone approaching Area H for a **Spot Hidden** roll:
- **Success:** the floor in front of the rubble comprises old wood covered in a layer of dust. A careful examination reveals the rotten wood hides a deep pit. The hazardous area can safely be traversed if suitable precautions are taken, such as tying guide ropes, or using timber from another part of the mine to bridge the area (no roll required).
- **Failure:** the first scout-investigator stepping on this area without taking precautions immediately falls through the rotten boards into a pit, some 10 feet (3


**CHAPTER 6** **126**

> — TREASURE OF THE SECRET WAY

m) deep, and suffers 1D6 damage—halve the damage if the scout makes a successful **Jump** roll.

The debris beyond the pit appears to block the way; however, on closer inspection, the scouts can squeeze through to the area beyond (see **Area I** , following).

### Area I: the Secret Vault

The first scout with a light source to squeeze through into this area glimpses a golden shimmering off the walls. When fully lit, the thick vein of gold running through the walls gleams brightly. The 8 inch (20 cm) wide vein runs in a seam some 7 feet (2 m) long. Two other notable items populate the small area:

- A skeleton with tattered clothes (belonging to the long-dead Heinrich Strauss). In his final days, after finding only fool’s gold, Strauss’ remaining partner, **Jedediah Brumsted** (page 135) clubbed Strauss over the head and blocked him up in this area. So, Strauss spent his final days starving here, alone in the darkness. Brumsted abandoned the mine and spread lies about Strauss’ demise.
- The second item is a tattered leather journal that belonged to Strauss. The contents are in German and difficult to decipher, especially near the end when he was writing in near darkness. Scoutinvestigators with Language (German) can spend a few hours studying to get **Handout: Treasure 3** . Otherwise, wait until later, with more time and a German-to-English dictionary.

- Someone with the Reading Badge (page 33) or the **Signals and Codes Badge** (page 33) can read a few key phrases—enough to know the journal’s writer was betrayed by a man named Brumsted.

**Keeper note:** the gold in this area is fool’s gold (iron pyrite) and worthless.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** Silas Ardmore arrives to harass the scoutinvestigators—see **Confrontation** , nearby.
- **Obvious:** spending five or more minutes in Area I summons **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** (page 128).

### Meeting Ardmore

Keepers can choose how to handle potential meetings with Ardmore, who could be encountered in the mine or at his campsite.

- If Ardmore knows the scouts are approaching, he attempts to hide—ask the scouts to make a **Spot Hidden** roll: with a Hard success, they spot him; otherwise, Ardmore remains hidden and attempts to spook them—choose a **Spooky Event** (page 122).
- If the scouts have just found the “gold” seam in **Area I** , Ardmore takes this opportunity to scare them off—see **Confrontation** (following).
- Ardmore attempts to menace or threaten the scouts with violence.
- Ardmore brokers a deal with the scouts to work together and split the gold. It occurs to Ardmore that the scouts are smaller than him and could squeeze through some of the small spaces in the mine. He plans on double-crossing them, of course.

Ultimately, none of these really change the outcome as the scouts soon face **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** (page 128).

### Confrontation

The scouts have little time to evaluate their find in Area I, as Ardmore chooses this moment to try to scare off the interlopers—now, they have “helped him” find the gold. On hearing the scouts talking about gold, he squeezes through the rubble and enters the chamber—in doing this, he unwittingly causes **Baba Itzak’s Brooch** (page 114) to slip off and fall to the ground.

On entering, he sees the gold seam, and a wicked look crosses his face. He doesn’t want to kill the scouts, but he is not averse to hitting them with a stick and yelling heinous threats. A selection of options for what happens are below.

- During the scene, ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll: success notes **Baba Itzak’s Brooch** (page 114) lying on the ground by the entrance.

### _**scouts Flee**_

If the scouts flee, they soon hear screams, as Ardmore is attacked by **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** (page 131). As he no longer wears **Baba Itzak’s Brooch** , he is defenseless against the angry spirit. Should the scouts return later, they find Ardmore’s corpse, horror frozen on his gaunt face—the ghost used up its magic points, so it cannot return for several hours.

### _**fighting Ardmore**_

The scouts may attempt an Assisted Fighting Maneuver (page 38), perhaps to restrain Ardmore or push him into **Area H: The Pit** (page 125). If Heinrich is getting the better of the scouts, have **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** **127**

### Chapter 6

(see following) appear and attack Ardmore. Probably, the **Scouts Flee** (preceding) at the sight of the ghost attacking Heinrich; otherwise, the ghost may target them too—see **Fighting the Ghost** , nearby.

If the scouts get an opportunity to interrogate Ardmore, he admits to trying to scare them with noises and lights. If they remain near “Strauss’ gold” in **Area I** (page 127), **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** (following) (re)appears to unleash its fury—as the scouts talk to Ardmore, his face pales, and his eyes widen in terror as the ghost appears behind them! See **Fighting the Ghost** , nearby.

### Running the Scene

The two most likely outcomes when encountering the ghost are: **Flee the Ghost** (nearby) or **Fighting the Ghost** (following) or both! Fighting a ghost is tricky, as the scouts soon find out. Strauss’ strong tie to the vein of fool’s gold means he does not pursue anyone outside the mine, and always returns to **Area I** (page 127).

- If scouts have time to gather their wits, ask for an **INT** roll (see **Leap of Logic** , page 47): with success, they realize Strauss’ ghost remains near the gold.
- Keepers can, optionally, have the ghost keep to **Area I** (page 127). While this makes the ghost less of a threat, it also shortens the playing time for those nearing the close of their session.

### Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost

Anyone spending five or more minutes in **Area I** (page 127) sees a strange purple mist begin forming in the chamber’s center. The mist takes on a menacing shape, forming a humanoid with spectral claws. Then, a ghastly skeletal face bursts forth and shrieks at everyone. The mist quickly reforms in the shape of an old miner, wielding a pickaxe.

- Seeing the ghost triggers a **Cool** roll.

### Fighting the Ghost

The ghost makes attacks using an opposed **POW** roll and manifests as swipes with a spectral pickaxe:

- If Strauss’ ghost wins the opposed POW roll: the target feels the pickaxe stab coldly into them—even through it’s not physical—losing 2D10 points of POW. Should a scout reach zero POW, they collapse unconscious and cannot be woken until they have been removed from the mine. POW lost to the ghost is recovered in the **Rewards** section (page 130).
- If Strauss’ ghost loses the opposed POW roll: the pickaxe misses—or passes clean through—and the ghost loses 1D8 magic points. When the ghost reaches zero magic points, it vanishes and cannot return for at least six hours.
- A scout-investigator wearing **Baba Itzak’s Brooch** (page 114) gains one bonus die on a **POW** roll versus the ghost’s attacks.
- If scout-investigators have **Banish the Visitor** (see **Boxcar Jim’s Useful Items** , page 63), they can cast it as a group to trap Strauss’ ghost in the corked glass bottle (as given to them by Boxcar Jim). Award one bonus die if casting in **Area I** (page 127).

Keeper note: the Cheating Death rule (page 42) can save scout-investigators from certain death. Also, note that the glass bottle containing the ghost can prove useful in the final scenes of the following scenario, **Shadow Over Westhaven** (page 132).

### Flee the Ghost

The scout-investigators run. Assuming they still have a light source, they need only win an opposed **DEX** roll versus the ghost’s DEX 45. Any who fail may be
attacked by the ghost. After each such attack, a scout may try to flee again, calling for another opposed **DEX** roll. If working together to help a friend out, Keepers may optionally grant one bonus die to the scout’s DEX roll.

Once each scout has succeeded, they have escaped the ghost and left the mine.

### Conclusion: We Are Rich!

Several possible outcomes follow, depending upon the scout-investigators’ actions.

- **Partial success (with one of DEX or INT):** they seal the mine, but all scouts must make a **Luck** roll to avoid falling rocks (1D4 damage).
- **Failure:** either the dynamite doesn’t go off or only partially blocks the passage.
- **Fumble:** the dynamite explodes while the scouts are in range; everyone makes a **Luck** roll: success inflicts 1D4+1 damage; failure 1D10 damage.

### _**run Away**_

They leave the old mine and go home.

### Strauss Is No More?

If the scouts dealt with Strauss’ ghost, they may now revisit **Area I** (page 127).

### _**blow It Up!**_

The scout-investigators could use the dynamite from **Area F** (page 124) to seal the mine.

- Ask the scout-investigator setting the sweating sticks for a combined **DEX** and **INT** roll (one dice roll compared to both characteristics).
- **Success (with both DEX and INT):** they safely seal the mine.
- Those inspecting the vein of “gold” should make an **Appraise** , Hard **Natural World** , or **Geology** roll. Consider rolling on the player’s behalf and keeping the result concealed, only telling the player the outcome—that makes the investigator’s assumptions more uncertain.
- **Success:** disappointingly, the gold is a pocket of iron pyrite, “fool’s gold,” and of no value.
- **Failure:** gold is gold, right!? Later, when they try to use or spend the gold, they learn the terrible truth.


### Chapter 6

Discovering the “gold” isn’t gold makes the fate of Heinrich Strauss and his mining crew even more tragic.

If they tell the authorities what happened—or at least a version of it—a search party returns to reclaim any bodies.

If scouts tied up Ardmore:

- If the knot-tier has the **Knot-Tying Badge** (page 32), he is securely tied and cannot escape.
- If they don’t have the badge, then ask for a **Luck** roll: failure has Ardmore escape, perhaps troubling the scouts again in the future. Otherwise, he is brought back to town and questioned. He has a poor reputation, and his efforts to incriminate the scouts fail.

### Rewards

The scout-investigators gain the following:

- Choose any new **Ability Badge** (page 30).
- POW lost to the ghost is recovered, back to their normal value, over the next week or so.

Grant a **Scout-Investigator Development Phase** (page 47).
