### Chapter 7: Shadow over Westhaven

This scenario is playable by up to six scoutinvestigators and may take several sessions T to complete. If part of the Westhaven Campaign, it works best as the conclusion.

The scout-investigators spend a weekend camping at Muskrat Lake. Soon, their pleasant adventure in New Hampshire’s Green Mountains turns to horror. First, when their scout leader is murdered, and second, when the scout-investigators return to Westhaven, they find it has undergone a dreadful transformation.

### Keeper Information

Jedediah Brumsted was a wealthy merchant and resident of Westhaven in the 19 [th] century. Brumsted devoted himself to the Windwalker, a Mythos deity of the frozen north. He needed a quiet place to worship, so he built a hunting lodge at Muskrat Lake, New Hampshire, some 100 miles (161 km) from Westhaven. The Windwalker promised great power, but Brumsted died before he could realize his dreams; he was buried at his lodge.

Brumsted’s descendants, Gustaf and Sydney, live on a farm south of town. The pair scratch out a living as best they can while serving as murderous acolytes of the Windwalker. They know little of Jedediah‘s story—whom they refer to as “grandfather,” though he’s really their great-greatgrandfather. They frequently use his old cabin for dark ceremonies and blood sacrifices.

Local cult leader, **Thomas Cline** (page 60), learned about Jedediah and convinced the siblings that he can unite their “grandfather” with the Windwalker; as Brumsted’s heirs, Gustaf and Sydney can inherit the Windwalker’s blessings. The first part is true: Cline is aiding Brumsted to ascend and join with the Windwalker; however, Cline will inherit the Windwalker’s blessing, not Brumsted’s descendants. Brumsted’s corpse has been dug up and moved to Westhaven in preparation for a magical ritual to summon the Windwalker.

Cline knows the scout-investigators may interfere, so he keeps the meddling kids away from Westhaven by orchestrating a camping trip to Muskrat Lake! Cline’s own daughter, Loretta, belongs to **The Stumbling Moose Squad** (page 65)—and he sends her to keep her out of harm’s way too. Cline is unaware that Muskrat Lake is haunted by monsters created by the murderous Sydney and Gustaf.

Scout leader **Paul Glenn** (page 56) eagerly accepted Cline’s offer of a weekend stay at a lakeside lodge. The trip consists of two overnight stays, during which Gustaf and Sydney act as lodge caretaker and cook.

In **Part One: Lakeside Horror** (page 136), two NPC (non-player character) scouts go missing on Saturday morning; the scout-investigators are sent to look for them. Later, when the scout leader discovers the bus sabotaged, tempers fray, and Paul Glenn is murdered. Scout-investigators must contend not only with the murderous Sydney and Gustaf, but also some terrifying, living wicker constructs.

Before they depart, scouts may search the lodge and discover Jedediah Brumsted’s now empty
grave in the cellar—clearly, they need to get back to Westhaven to stop worse horrors from happening!

In **Part Two: Hands of Winter** (page 150), the scout-investigators return to find Westhaven changed. Cline has the whole town under his spell: curious wooden towers are being constructed, while townsfolk hide in the shadows, and winter arrived early. Cline reanimates Jedediah Brumsted’s corpse in preparation for the scenario’s climax: the arrival of the Windwalker!

**Keeper note:** if relocating to Arkham, no major changes are needed for **Part One: Lakeside Horror** . In **Part Two: Hands of Winter** , adjust the duration of the Windwalker’s effect on the city (see **Adjusting for Arkham** , page 69).

### Involving the Scout-investigators

The scenario begins with the scout-investigators singing as they drive to Muskrat Lake in New Hampshire for a weekend camping trip.


- **Playing as a stand-alone scenario:** any scoutinvestigators can be used.
- **Playing as a part four of the Westhaven Campaign:** two years have passed since **Treasure of the Secret Way** (page 112), and it is now early September 1924; the scout-investigators should now be **Wardens** (page 29).

### Dramatis Personae

**Keeper note: Shadow Over Westhaven** takes place two years after **Treasure of the Secret Way** , and thus recurring NPCs’ ages have been increased accordingly. Also, to avoid confusion, we refer to Jedediah Brumsted as “Brumsted,” and his descendants as “Gustaf” and “Sydney” throughout.

### **paul Glenn,** _**age 60**_ **,** _**adult Scout Leader**_

- See **Paul Glenn** , page 56.

### **gustaf Brumsted,** _**age 39**_ **,** _**cultist and Murderous Brother**_

Gustaf is a descendant of Jedediah Brumsted, and refers to Brumsted as _“Grandpappy.”_ He and his sister, Sydney, live at his farm on the south edge of Westhaven, rearing pigs. As cultists, Gustaf and Sydney have been kidnapping and murdering people at Brumsted’s hunting lodge; and through magical rites have reanimated corpses, known as **Wicker Ones** (page 142), to do their bidding.

Prior to the scenario, **Thomas Cline** (page 60) manipulated Gustaf with promises of greater occult power; however, Gustaf and Sydney have their own plan: unleash wicker ones on the scouts, then attempt to ascend with the Windwalker.

- **Description:** tall, broad-shouldered, slightly stooped, with a thin mustache and oiled hair.
- **Traits:** a compulsive liar and a crook.
- **Roleplaying hooks:** follows Cline’s order to help with the scout camp, but if Gustaf and Sydney have their way, the scouts never go home!


### Shadow over Westhaven


### **sydney Brumsted,** _**age 34**_ **,** _**cultist and Murderous Sister**_

Sydney also descends from Jedediah Brumsted. This miscreant lives with her brother, Gustaf, on the edge of Westhaven, keeping pigs. They both worship the Windwalker. Together, they murder people at the old lodge to create **Wicker Ones** (pages 142) and show their deity devotion.

Thomas Cline manipulated her brother, Gustaf, into doing his bidding, and Sydney happily follows suit; chaperoning scouts at the lodge provides the pair with more potential sacrifices for the Windwalker!

- **Description:** tall, wears a dirty leather apron, as well as thick-lensed, black-rimmed glasses.
- **Traits:** rarely speaks, and when she does, she speaks with a thick, rural accent.
- **Roleplaying hooks:** the scout-investigators meet Sydney at the lodge. Cline naïvely tasked her with keeping his daughter, Loretta, safe, not realizing Sydney plans to murder all the scouts—Loretta among them.

### **Jedediah Brumsted,** _**age 143**_ **,** _**reanimated Cultist**_

Having first encountered the Windwalker when traversing the northern wilds, Brumsted spent much of his life seeking lore and exploring evil rituals. The Windwalker’s blessing granted him long life, but, eventually, he died and was buried before he could ascend to join his deity.

Through research and arcane study, Thomas Cline discovered Brumsted’s resting place; he now plans to use the old man’s corpse to form his own bond with the Windwalker.

- **Description:** a horrifyingly withered, walking cadaver. His skin is hardened and leathery and stained a deep maroon-color. A patchwork of intertwined twigs is woven into his chest—viewers can see his beating heart through the laced twigs. Viewing the sorcerer for the first time requires a **Cool** roll—see Brumsted’s profile in **Characters & Monsters** (page 163).
- **Traits:** his movements are stiff and jerky.
- **Roleplaying hooks:** Brumsted hates the living and does not hesitate to kill if his plans go awry.

**135** **CHAPTER 7**

### Leads: Obscure & Obvious Clues

At the end of key sections, we list “leads” to help Keepers run scenarios smoothly. This provides clear routes for scout-investigators to follow in terms of clues and plot progression. The leads tell Keepers which scene or event comes next, navigating the path the story can take from here.

- If the lead is labeled “obvious”—make scoutinvestigators aware of it as the scene progresses. They usually do not require a skill roll. Obvious leads are essential to the plot.
- If the lead is labeled “obscure”—the clue usually requires some form of specific action or a skill roll to access the information; or it may be available from a more easily accessible alternative source. Usually, obscure clues are not necessary to move the scenario forward.

### Part One: Lakeside Horror

### Start: Wheels on the Bus

Read aloud or paraphrase the following:

> _It’s a warm day in early September. You worked hard recently, organizing community events, like last week’s trash pickup in the graveyard. Now, you are eagerly awaiting your rewards: a three-day camping trip to New Hampshire’s Green Mountains. Your squad, together with the Stumbling Moose squad, are ready to depart for Muskrat Lake._

> _A bus pulls up to the sidewalk, and scout leader Glenn steps from the opening doors, followed by a tall man._

“Troops,” _says Glenn,_ “meet Mister Gustaf Brumsted.” _The man next to Glenn nods a welcome._ “He’s agreed to help chaperone this weekend, and I expect you to follow his orders just as you’d follow mine. We’ve got a long trip, so let’s load our gear and get going.”

The three-hour drive takes you north across the New Hampshire state line. The scout-investigators share the bus this trip with **The Stumbling Moose Squad** (page 65), with whom they share a friendly rivalry. The giddy, boisterous mood fills the coach. Gustaf smiles and nods a lot but says little. Small villages and tended fields pass by. As the final town slips out of view, the last half-hour of the trip winds uphill through dense forest, dotted with blue-tinted bare rock.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** proceed to **Arriving at Muskrat Lake** (following).

### Arriving at Muskrat Lake

Muskrat Lake sits in a large, heavily forested depression surrounded by steep wooded hills on all sides. A wooden lodge sits facing a substantial clearing on the shore of the shining lake. Golden sunlight filters through a mix of oak and pine trees. Nearby, an exercise field features archery targets, a flagpole, and horseshoe pits.

Paul Glenn parks the bus and tells the scouts to gather in the dining room.

- Give the players the Muskrat Lake map (player version).

### The Lodge

The lodge comprises a single-story log cabin, 20 by 50 feet (6 by 15 m), with a wooden porch. The front door leads to a sizable dining room with two wooden tables and benches. A stone fireplace sits on the right, with a stuffed boar’s head projecting from the chimney. A painting of Muskrat Lake hangs on one wall. The front windows offer a captivating lake view, while the rear ones look out onto the dense forest behind.

A corridor leads to a trophy room and a library, with the kitchen and pantry beyond. The rustic kitchen has several counters, an assortment of pots and pans, a hand-pump water spigot, a wood-burning stove. The pantry has shelves of canned goods and jarred vegetables and a door opening onto a back porch.

- Glenn offers a special thanks to Thomas Cline for the invitation to use the lodge, asking the scouts to give three cheers for their benefactor!

Glenn then orders the troop to unload the bus. Gustaf directs them on where to stow provisions and gear. Those entering the pantry meet a tall woman— **Sydney Brumsted** (page 135)—who wears a dirty leather apron. Sydney points at the shelves and barks orders if anyone puts provisions in the “wrong” place.


- Sydney loudly asks, _“Which one you young ’uns be Low-retta?”_ After Loretta is pointed out, Sydney gazes at her intently, silently memorizing her features, much to Loretta’s discomfort.

### The Tents

They must now erect enough two-person tents to sleep all the scouts. The old, worn fabric is drab, green army canvas.

- Have scout-investigators decide with whom they share.

### Row Your Boat

With unloading accomplished and tents set up, Glenn suggests a boat race. Sunlight twinkles down through the trees, while the lake ripples with blue and white flashes. Two birch-bark canoes lie on their sides near the lake’s dock. Two flags are visible on the lake’s far side: a red one for the scout-investigator squad, and a blue one for the Stumbling Moose Squad. The winner returns with their flag first. The losers must wash dishes after dinner.

Each scout gets a paddle. The lake feels refreshingly cold. While Glenn directs the race, Gustaf stands at the front of the lodge, smoking a hornpipe and watching.

**Keeper note:** Sissy Shuemaker and Betty Bartlett of **The Stumbling Moose Squad** (page 65) _must_ share a tent.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** scout-investigators may **Search the Lodge** (page 147).
- **Obvious:** otherwise, proceed to **Row Your Boat** (following).
- To decide the winner, have one scout-investigator roll **Pilot (Boat)** , while the others roll **STR** .
- Have the players roll one at a time. Use each result to help improvise a narration of the race: success sees the scout-investigators pull ahead; failure has the Stumbling Moose Squad gain or take the lead.
- Total the successes and failures rolled (do not roll for the NPCs): if the scout-investigators rolled more successes, they win the race. If the


scout-investigators rolled more failures, they lose. In case of a draw, decide with a group Luck roll.

### _**leads**_

**• Obvious:** Proceed to **Campfire Tales** (nearby).

### Options

The players likely follow the series of events outlined in the following sections— **Campfire Tales** (following) to **Return to the Lodge** (page 144)—before they **Search the Lodge** (page 147). If players search much earlier, perhaps on the first night, especially if they distrust Gustaf and Sydney, these events still occur; adjust their timing to better fit the players’ actions.

In some cases, the scout-investigators may not actually be present; therefore, they could miss clues. Where appropriate, ensure such clues reach the players via other methods. If a group splits up, try to alternate between the two groups, building the story step-by-step; allow scout-investigators to meet up at a suitable point to share what they learned.

### Campfire Tales

After dinner, dishes are cleared and washed by the losing team. Shortly after sunset, Glenn has the troop build a huge campfire and end the evening singing songs and telling stories. After Glenn finishes a suitably creepy story, Gustaf pipes up. While staring into the fire, he tells the following tale:

> _“My old grandpappy, he wus one o’ the founders o’ Westhaven. Nigh-on 300 years ago, afore folk had heered o’ Westhaven, there was a trading post that catered to trappers ’n’ hunters—mountain men they called ’em. Grandpappy went north with some of ’em._

> _“They wus supposed to return arter a few weeks. Instead, many months later, one of them ill-fated trappers, stumbled into the tradin’ post, all wild-eyed and disheveled. It was my grandpappy! Suffrin’ from malnourishment, he said his group was set upon by a terrible spirit as drove many of ’em mad. He claimed he found one of his last comrades a-feasting on the remains of another and fled._

> _“Now, back then, Westhaven’s leader wus Samuel Minter, who claimed to be a witch hunter, like them over in Salem. He accused grandpappy of bein’ a cannibal an’ a murderer_


### Shadow over Westhaven

> _an’ consortin’ with demons. Had him hung ’til ’e was dead, outside the south side o’ town.”_

Uncomfortable, Glenn butts in awkwardly, _“That’s enough of that one. Thank you, Gustaf.”_ Glenn orders the scouts to extinguish the fire and go to bed. The three adults sleep in the lodge; Gustaf and Sydney share a bedroom, with Glenn in the bedroom opposite. The scouts bed down in tents outside.

**Keeper note:** Gustaf’s story possesses some truth, as it describes the first contact between the Brumsted family and the Windwalker.

- Scout-investigators who found Samuel Minter’s book in **Tremors Below** (page 70) may recognize the name.

### Dead of Night

It’s the middle of the night, and the scout-investigators are assumed to be asleep.

- Call for a **Dodge** roll for the scout concerned.
- **Failure:** the scout suffers 1 damage, with a cut to the head.
- A Hard **Spot Hidden** roll works out that Gustaf is holding an engine part.
- The resulting altercation wakes everyone.
- If a scout is injured, Gustaf insists he was halfasleep and lashed out in the dark, thinking it was an intruder—Glenn knows it’s a lame story, but has little choice but accept it for now.
- If scouts draw Glenn’s attention to the distributor cap, he demands to know why Gustaf removed it. Gustaf spins a yarn about wandering vagrants stealing vehicles at night, and how he removed the cap to stop such an occurrence.
- Glenn clearly doesn’t believe Gustaf but is at a loss for what to do. He orders everyone back to their tents, saying to Gustaf that they will talk about it in the morning.

### _**engine Part**_

- Ask for a **Listen** roll: success hears a metallic click, waking the scout(s).
- A quick look reveals a dark figure entering the lodge. The scouts can’t identify the person, but they are carrying something football-sized.

**Keeper note:** the metallic click was the bus’ engine hood closing.

Should any scout-investigators hurry over and enter the (unlocked) lodge, they find Gustaf in the dining room, removing his boots. If asked, he claims to have been outside answering a “call of nature.”

- If scout-investigators get a look at the engine part, ask for a **Mechanical Repair** roll: success identifies it as a distributor cap; the bus won’t run without it.
- If they inspect the bus engine, ask for a **Mechanical Repair** roll: success notes the distributor cap is missing; the bus won’t run without it.

**Keeper note:** Gustaf expects the **Wicker Ones** (page 142) to abduct some scouts in the night; he sabotaged the bus to prevent anyone escaping or going for help.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** proceed to **Saturday Morning** (following).
- The room is dark, ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll—with one penalty die if they lack a light source.
- **Success:** Gustaf is concealing something. Do they dare confront him?

### _**if Confronting Gustaf**_

Gustaf scowls and whispers threateningly for them to leave: _“You don’t want to wake your scout leader, do you?”_ **Keeper note:** Gustaf doesn’t want Paul Glenn awakened, either; he wants solitude to hide the bus’ distributor cap beneath the trapdoor in the **Trophy Room** (page 147), and then return to bed. If scouts persist and further provoke Gustaf, he grows angry and swings the distributor cap at a scout’s head!


### Chapter 7

### Saturday Morning

Paul Glenn wakes at dawn and blows reveille on a bugle. All scouts are expected to rush to the lawn and line up in formation. Gustaf is dressed and standing next to Glenn. Two Stumbling Moose Squad members are missing: Sissy Shuemaker and Betty Bartlett. Glenn sends a scout-investigator to fetch them.

Approaching the tent, the scout-investigator sees a huge tear on the reverse side. Sissy and Betty are gone, but all their gear, including uniforms and shoes remain. Strewn inside the tent are broken twigs and dried leaves, as if someone shook a dried-up bush inside. Bits of twigs even got stuck in the tent’s torn fabric.

- Ask for a **Track** roll: success notes a series of impressions in the earth found by the ripped tent. They look like brush marks and scuffles, as if someone raked the ground with branches. The scuff marks continue to the tree line and then vanish; however, several fresh scratches and gouges mark a nearby tree, traveling up from the base to some branches around 20 feet (6 m) higher.

### Search Parties

Glenn forms two search parties to scour the woods for the missing girls. The scout-investigators form their own group, while Glenn leads a second group consisting of the remaining NPC scouts. Gustaf and Sydney stay behind in case Sissy and Betty show up. Glenn suggests the groups search for four hours, then return to the lodge.

**Keeper note:** talk about the previous night’s events is put aside while the girls are missing.

The challenging terrain includes many mountain streams flowing down the steep, rocky ravines. To capture the difficulty of traveling, ask scoutinvestigators to make two rolls, spaced out over the course of their journey:

- First, make a **Climb** roll to scale a steep ravine or rocky cliff.
- Then, make a **Jump** roll to cross a stream (or a **DEX** roll to wade through it).
- **Failure of either roll:** make a **Feeling Sore** roll (see **Sore** , page 46) to determine how they are affected. Are they okay? Do they suffer bruises and tiredness?


### Shadow over Westhaven

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** two missing scouts must be found. Do the scout-investigators focus on **Searching the Woods** (following)?
- **Obvious:** do they focus on **Searching Around the Lake** (nearby)?

### Searching the Woods

No tracks mar the ground to follow, so the scoutinvestigators must walk through the woods calling out for their missing friends. Clever scout-investigators might look for more scratches in the trees and try to follow those.

recognize the scout-investigators; an abundance of scratches populate her skin, concentrated around her arms, waist, and shoulders. A large bruise covers her right shoulder, and several smaller bruises cover her arms and legs.

- If asked what happened, Sissy’s responds in a whimpering voice: _“The woods took me. The trees. Took me.”_
- If they try to retrace Sissy’s footsteps, ask for a **Track** roll: success finds a **Bundle** (following).

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** Betty Bartlett is still missing.
- Ask for a **Track** roll: success follows marks left by **Wicker Ones** (page 142) leading to a **Bundle** (nearby).
- **Obvious:** do the scout-investigators return to the lodge with Sissy—see **Return to the Lodge** (page 144)—or do they **Continue the Search** (page 144)?
- **Obscure:** if they found the **Bundle** (following).

### _**leads**_

- **Obscure:** if they successfully used **Track** —see **Bundle** , nearby.
- **Obvious:** otherwise, after a few hours searching, they find the **Wandering Child** (nearby).

### Searching Around the Lake

The forest meets the lake’s edge, making scoutinvestigators get wet feet if they slip their way around the shoreline. They may wish to use a rowboat instead to get a better look at the area.

- Ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll: success spies a muddy, trampled landing area with a trail.
- An hour’s walk up the trail takes the scoutinvestigators to the **Woodland Altar** (page 143).

### _**leads**_

- **Obscure:** if they follow the trail, it leads to the **Woodland Altar** (page 143).
- **Obvious:** Otherwise, regardless of their dice rolls, after a few hours searching, they find the **Wandering Child** (following).

### Wandering Child

**Keeper note:** the scout-investigators find Sissy automatically, no roll required.

Dropped by her midnight abductor, a shivering Sissy Shuemaker stumbles from the woods, moaning softly. Sissy stares vacantly and doesn’t seem to

### Bundle

At the foot of a tree is a riot of scattered branches and leaves. Looking up, wedged in the higher branches, close to the tree trunk, is what looks like a bundle of sticks, like a big squirrel’s nest.

- Ask for a **Natural World** roll: success indicates it’s too big for a squirrel nest.

### _**climbing Up**_

- Those attempting to climb up to the bundle should make a **Climb** roll.
- **Success:** they scale the tree.
- As the scout gets close, the bundle begins to move, see **Combat With a Wicker One** , page 142.
- **Failure:** the scout can’t get a firm grip on the trunk. Perhaps a friend can assist by helping them to reach for higher handhold—allow a pushed **Climb** roll.
- **Fumble or failed pushed roll:** the scout-investigator climbs part way and becomes stranded. The wicker one senses the scout and grabs at them—if grabbed, the wicker one tries to carry them off through the treetops (see **Combat With a Wicker One** , page 142).

### _**cautious Approach**_

Should scout-investigators wish to employ more caution, they may attempt to dislodge the bundle from the tree with a long stick.

- Ask for a **STR** roll: success knocks the bundle to the ground where it unravels, suffering no damage.


### Chapter 7

- The bundle unravels into a shortish figure made of sticks. The stick man proceeds to attack the scout-investigators (see **Combat With a Wicker One** , following).

### Wicker Ones

Gustaf and Sydney spawned these monsters in their midnight rituals at their **Woodland Altar** (page 143). A wicker one is created by wrapping a human corpse in interlacing branches and vines. The skin and heart are removed prior to wrapping, and the heart replaced with a diminutive fetish-like object. A spell then animates the creation. They do not speak, being silent and creepy horrors that haunt the forest.

The wicker ones are dormant during daylight hours, only acting if disturbed. See **Wicker Ones** (page 162) for the profile.

### Combat with a Wicker One

The wicker ones fall dormant during daylight hours, but if disturbed, they attack—however, all their combat rolls are made with a penalty die during daylight hours, and their MOV rate is reduced to 7 (see **Wicker Ones** , page 162).

The wicker ones aim to grab the scout-investigators and carry them off. If outmatched and injured, wicker ones attempt to climb a tree to escape.

- Call for a **Cool** roll when encountering wicker ones.

Keeper note: the optional Luck Spending rule (page 41) gives players the chance to adjust poor skill rolls; should events lead to the worst, the **Cheating Death** rule (page 42) can save their scout-investigator from certain death.

### Studying a Lifeless Wicker One

If scout-investigators kill a wicker one, they see that something hides within the woven branches, and they realize it’s a human corpse! Hundreds of short

branches and vines woven around and through its bones form a macabre scarecrow, with branches growing out of the mouth and eye sockets.

If they unknot or cut through the branches, they find the corpse’s heart removed, and an odd, fetishlike construct stuffed inside the cavity. This construct consists of a knot of twine wrapped around a smaller heart, that of a bird or rodent.

**Keeper note:** the first wicker one encountered contains the corpse of missing Albany scout, Squire Cuthbert—for information about Cuthbert, see the **Library** (page 148).

- Removing the fetish-construct breaks the spell animating the wicker one; it falls apart, becoming merely sticks and bones.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** regardless of their dice rolls, they soon find Sissy Shuemaker (see **Wandering Child** , page 141).
- **Obvious:** if Sissy has been found, one child remains missing—do the scout-investigators **Return to the Lodge** (page 144), or do they **Continue the Search** (page 144) for Betty?

### Woodland Altar

Near the trail’s end, the way is blocked by a big ball of branches and vines—a curled-up, dormant wicker one. Beyond lies a small clearing.

- If the scout-investigators skirt around the dormant wicker one, ask for a **Stealth** roll.
- **Success:** they avoid disturbing the wicker one.
- **Failure:** it animates and attacks (see **Combat With a Wicker One** , page 142).

### The Small Clearing

The air here feels eerily cold. Frosty cobwebs hang between the branches of dark trees. In the center of the clearing, seven wooden stakes in the ground make a circle some 20 feet (6 m) across. Each post stands tall as a person and around 1 foot (30 cm) thick. The posts prickle with nails, seemingly hammered in at random points.

**Keeper note:** Gustaf and Sydney murder people here and use their corpses to create wicker ones. While nothing more occurs here for the scout-investigators, the clues point to evil taking place. Keepers with “gorehound” players may want to add a severed hand, attached by a nail to one of the stakes—calling for a **Cool** roll.

- A search finds dark stains (old, dried blood) on the posts and torn, half-buried bloodstained clothing. The clothing includes part of a scout’s uniform.


### Chapter 7

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** regardless of their dice rolls, they soon find Sissy (see **Wandering Child** , page 141).
- **Obvious:** if Sissy has been found, one child remains missing—do the scout-investigators **Return to the Lodge** (nearby), or do they **Continue the Search** (following) for Betty?

### Night in the Woods

Scout-investigators are likely tired and weary.

- Their stomachs are empty—refer to **Feeling Hungry** (see **Hunger** , page 45).
- If scout-investigators have not yet visited the **Woodland Altar (** page 143), they stumble upon it at sunset.

### Continue the Search

The scout-investigators continue their search; soon, night draws near. Although they found Sissy, Betty remains missing. They can **Return to the Lodge** , (nearby) with Sissy or push deeper into the forest.

### Glenn to the Rescue

As darkness falls, the temperature drops, and the woods become even more creepy. Walking becomes more challenging as hungry scouts stumble over unseen tree roots, rocks, and fallen branches. Suddenly, they hear something!

- Either way, ask for a **Navigate** roll:
- **Success:** players choose—go deeper into the forest— see **Finding Betty** (following) or **Return to the Lodge** (nearby).
- **Failure:** they become lost—they can either push the roll or spend a **Night in the Woods** (nearby).
- **Fumble or a failed pushed roll:** they truly get lost; darkness begins to fall. Ask for a **Cool** roll, for **Feeling Lost** (see **Lost** , page 45). The scouts must spend a **Night in the Woods** (nearby).
- Ask for a group **Luck** roll.
- **Success:** they hear scout leader Glenn’s voice beckoning—he saves them from spending any more time alone in the dark forest.
- **Failure:** a wicker one loudly crashes in among them and attacks (see **Combat With a Wicker One** , page 142). Now night, the monster does not have a penalty die on its actions. After 3 rounds—or if the Keeper feels appropriate—Glenn appears from the shadows and lands a killing blow (no roll required).

### Finding Betty

- Searching for Betty Bartlett, ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll.
- **Success:** they find Betty.
- **Failure:** they spot a bundle (a dormant wicker one) up another tree—if disturbed, it attacks (see **Combat With a Wicker One** , page 142).

Betty acts similarly to Sissy, staring vacantly, withdrawn. Small scratches and bruises cover her skin, concentrated around her arms, waist, and shoulders. Like Sissy, she remembers little and just wants to go home.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** regardless, scout leader Glenn leads the scouts back to the lodge (see **Return to the Lodge** , following).

### Return to the Lodge

- If the scouts returned promptly to the lodge, it is now early afternoon.
- If Glenn rescued them (per a **Night in the Woods** , above), it is now night.

**Keeper note:** if scout-investigators fail to find Betty, she can either be found by the other search team or remain forever lost.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** scout-investigators can **Return to the Lodge** (nearby)
- **Obvious:** if lost, they must spend a **Night in the Woods** (following).

Scout leader Glenn gathers the scouts inside the dining room for a conference. Betty Bartlett may still be missing, while Sissy is in shock. He tells them all to sit while he stands near the fireplace. He tells them he must go get help, but to his dismay, the bus won’t start.

- If scout-investigators accuse Gustaf of bus sabotage, an argument erupts. Gustaf becomes mad.
- If scout-investigators bring up the **Woodland Altar** (page 143), an angry Gustaf laughs maniacally: _“Ha! You like that place? You’ll all end up there soon enough!”_


### Shadow over Westhaven

When the Keeper decides—as tempers rise—Sydney suddenly appears, rushing in from the hallway behind Glenn and plunges a meat cleaver into his neck. This all happens before the scout-investigators have a chance to intervene. Glenn’s legs buckle, and he falls to his knees, a geyser of blood erupting from his neck!

- Call for a **Cool** roll upon witnessing Glenn’s attack.
- If scout-investigators decide to fight, Sydney trades blows for 1–2 rounds, but doesn’t want to kill the scouts now—assume she Dodges rather than fights back.
- Gustaf doesn’t attack, doing his best to drag Sydney away, out of the lodge to flee into the woods— heading for the **Woodland Altar** (page 143).
- Unless scouts overcome the siblings, they escape to the forest.
- A successful **First Aid** roll stabilizes Paul Glenn, but he remains unconscious for now.

**Keeper note:** the meat cleaver remains embedded in Glenn’s neck. It may come in handy later when opening the **Hidden Trapdoor** (page 147).

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** secure the doors and **Search the Lodge** (following).
- **Obvious:** leave the lodge and head home or seek help, see **Return to Westhaven** (page 149).
- **Obvious:** if they hide in the woods, they get herded back to the lodge by wicker ones.
- **Obvious:** Gustaf and Sydney’s trail is easy to follow (no roll); and the pair lead the scout-investigators to a confrontation at the **Woodland Altar** (page 143); here, they try to murder the scout-investigators to turn them into more wicker ones. The scouts may try to overcome them, or they can flee—Gustaf and Sydney do not pursue.

**145** **CHAPTER 7**


### Shadow over Westhaven

### Search the Lodge

Two short hallways lead left and right from the dining room. Down the left hallway are two **Bedrooms** (following), while the right hallway leads to the **Trophy Room** (below), **Library** (page 148), kitchen and pantry (see **The Lodge** , page 136). By the pantry, a set of stairs leads down to the **Root Cellar** (page 148).

### Bedrooms

The lodge features two bedrooms. The smaller room on the right currently hosts Glenn, while the room opposite is shared by Gustaf and Sydney. The two rooms contrast strongly: Glenn’s bed is neatly made, with his clothes hung up and folded; while Gustaf’s and Sydney’s beds are in disarray, with their clothes strewn on the floor.

- No important clues are in Gustaf and Sydney’s room, just bags containing their own clothing and a dog-eared copy of _Love Story_ , a popular pulpromance magazine of the period on a bedside table.

### Trophy Room

Trophies adorn this room’s walls, with brown-leather couches and chairs for seating. The floor is mostly covered by a bearskin rug. A painting on the right wall depicts an elderly Jedediah Brumsted standing in front of the lodge when it was first built. The artist painted “1873” at the bottom.

A stone fireplace takes up much of the left wall, with fire pokers on a stand. In the far corner, a wooden case with glass panels holds a 12-gauge double-barreled shotgun, along with five shotgun shells.

- The shotgun counts as a **Cumbersome Weapon** (page 40).
- If the room is searched, ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll— several items can be found.
- **Success:** a **Hidden Trapdoor** (following) hides under the bearskin rug.
- **Success:** something small and metal lies concealed in the ashes. If retrieved and wiped clean, it’s a Wayfarer Scout belt buckle, like the ones scoutinvestigators wear.
- **Hard Success:** a loose stone at the front of the fireplace is discovered—if shifted, it opens a secret door in the fireplace’s stonework, revealing a narrow, spiral staircase down to **The Secret Room** (page 149) in the root cellar.

**Keeper note:** the belt buckle belonged to Squire Cuthbert, one of the duo’s earlier victims; it alerts scout-investigators to the awful events taking place here. Note that a newspaper article relating to Squire Cuthbert can be found in the **Library** (page 148).


### _**hidden Trapdoor**_

Beneath the bearskin rug is a locked trapdoor.

- Ask for a **Locksmith** or Hard **Mechanical Repair** roll: success opens the trapdoor opens.
- Alternatively, a successful **STR** roll with a suitable lever (e.g., fire poker) breaks it, or a tool, (e.g., meat cleaver) smashes through the wood.
- Another option is the key in Gustaf’s pocket.

Opening the trapdoor reveals a 1 -yard/meter-square wooden compartment holding two hunting knives in sheaths and several stacks of $ 10 bills.


### Chapter 7

- The old bills date back to the 1860s and 1870s.
- The scout bus’ missing distributor cap is also in here.

If anyone thinks to smash through the bottom of the hidden compartment, they find **The Secret Room** (page 149) in the cellar; however, the 8 foot (2.4 m) drop down makes it easier to get in than get out.

### Library

A small bookcase holds dime novels, and dog-eared pulp magazines, and some larger ledgers and books. A faded and cracked red-leather chair sits in the near corner, while a rolltop desk and high-back chair stand against the far wall. On the desk are various receipts, bills, and a newspaper. The desk has one **Locked Drawer** (see below).

The following items are available without a skill roll:

- On the desk is a copy of the _Manchester Union Leader_, dated August 12, 1920, with the headline, “Local Boy Scout Missing.” The story explains how Squire Cuthbert, 17, of the Wayfarer Scout Troop of Albany, New York, remains missing, after getting lost while camping at Muskrat Lake. Mr. Brumsted organized an extensive search of the lake’s grounds.

- On the desk, the receipts, dated 1920, are for food and provisions.
- Beneath the receipts is a letter from scout leader Erica Billson of Albany. It thanks Mr. Brumsted for offering to let the Albany troop stay at Muskrat Lake, and says she looks forward to meeting the caretaker, Gustaf Brumsted.
- The rolltop desk has a locked drawer (see **Locked Drawer** , below).

**Keeper note:** the fate of poor Squire Cuthbert plays no further part in the scenario; it merely illustrates Gustaf and Sydney’s horrible crimes.

- If scout-investigators conduct a thorough search, ask for a **Spot Hidden** or **Library Use** roll—several objects can be found:
- **Success:** the bookshelf has an old manifest and cargo list from _The Profligate_ , dated 1824, and operated by Jedediah Brumsted; clearly, Brumsted bought and sold enslaved people.
- **Success:** the bookshelf has an accounting ledger for Bolton Worsted Mill (all decades old), owned by Jedediah Brumsted.
- **Hard success:** finds the key for the **Locked Drawer** (see below) on a small shelf in the back of the rolltop desk.
- **Success:** a photograph album, titled “The Brumsted Family Photography Journal.” Within is a picture of Jedediah Brumsted as an old man. It notes Brumsted died in 1897. The deaths of brothers, sisters, and several nieces and nephews are also noted. Looking through the journal, the last surviving family members appear to be Gustaf, born in 1885, and Sydney, born in 1890.

**Keeper note:** Jedediah Brumsted’s picture matches the portrait hanging in the **Trophy Room** (page 147)—it is unquestionably the same man.

### _**locked Drawer**_

If the drawer key is not found, scout-investigators can force it using a suitable tool.

- Ask for a **STR** roll: success splinters the wood around the lock allowing the drawer to be opened.

Inside the drawer are three penknives (1D3 damage), several old fountain pens, and a few dried-up jars of India ink, as well as a spare key for the trapdoor in the floor of the **Trophy Room** (page 147).

### Root Cellar

The staircase next to the pantry leads to a root cellar with stone walls. The scent of old socks and blocked drains fills the air. A pile of loose potatoes occupies one corner, with turnips in another.

- If scout-investigators already visited the **Recreation Room** (following), ask for an **INT** roll (see **Leap of Logic** , page 47): success notes the root cellar’s dimensions don’t match the recreation room supposedly on the other side of the interior wall; so, a hidden space may exist between the two!

### Recreation Room

The staircase beside the back bedroom leads to a recreation room. A dusty, old pool table and table tennis table stand in the middle of the room, with various camping and sporting equipment on the floor. Those wishing to arm themselves might be interested in the bow and arrows and pool cues:

- Two archery bows have 35 arrows each (1D4 damage; lightweight bows).


> — SHADOW OVER WESTHAVEN


- Pool cues make excellent improvised clubs (1D6 damage); they count as **Cumbersome Weapons** (page 40).
- If searching the room, ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll: success notes the cellar’s walls are painted a dull tan. The interior wall separating the recreation room from the root cellar consist of _brick_ rather than _stone_ .
- If scout-investigators already visited the **Root Cellar** (preceding), ask for an **INT** roll (see **Leap of Logic** , page 47): success notes the root cellar’s dimensions don’t match the recreation room supposedly on the other side of the interior wall; so, a hidden space may exist between the two!

**Keeper note:** the secret room between the Root Cellar and Recreation Room is accessible from the **Trophy Room** (page 147) directly above. Scout-investigators improvising a battering ram can knock a hole in one of the cellar’s internal walls to enter the **Secret Room** (following).

### The Secret Room

This cramped chamber reeks of sweat and old blood. Three pairs of manacles hang suspended from one wall, while a rough-hewn pine table holds an assortment of scalpels, pliers, and spools of barbed wire. Dirt and clutter line the edges of the room. A narrow, spiral staircase runs up to a door on the level above. Disturbingly, a 6 foot deep (1.8 m) hole in the middle of the earthen floor has freshly dug earth piled on either side. The hole measures 7 feet by 2 feet (2 m by 0.6 m).

**Keeper note:** until recently, this was a grave holding Jedediah **Laboratory** Brumsted’s coffin; it has been moved to the  (page 154) in Gustaf’s Farm in Westhaven.

- The spiral staircase connects with a secret door in the **Trophy Room** fireplace (page 147).
- Ask for a **Spot Hidden** roll: success spies a leather wallet lying beneath the table. Inside are two onedollar bills and a library card belonging to Squire Cuthbert—the missing scout mentioned in the _Manchester Union Leader_ (see **Library** , page 148).

**Keeper note:** if Gustaf and Sydney live, they go to the **Woodland Altar** (page 143) to pray to the Windwalker. Their pleas heard, the Windwalker arrives and grabs them up! They may get deposited later in Westhaven to further trouble the scouts (see **Gustaf & Sydney Reappear** , page 159).

### Return to Westhaven

The outlook seems bleak. Their leader is either dead or injured and unconscious. Gustaf and Sydney either disappeared or were handled. Getting back to Westhaven may be a challenge.


### Chapter 7

- If scout-investigators stay at the lodge, wicker ones trouble them the next night—clearly, Muskrat Lake is not safe.
- Optionally, if at night, on exiting the lodge, ask for a group **Luck** roll: failure leads to a wicker one spotting them and attacking.
- If the missing distributor cap has been found, ask for a **Mechanical Repair** roll: success gets the bus running—a scout-investigator can drive them all home.
- If needed, some old rusty bicycles are stored behind the lodge—one for each scout.
- The scouts could walk to the main road and hitch a lift from a delivery driver. Do they head home to Westhaven or contact the nearest police station (see following)?

### Contacting the New Hampshire Police

The community-minded scout-investigators may contact the police about recent events at Muskrat Lake, particularly if Gustaf and Sydney vanished after attacking Paul Glenn. If so, they immediately send officers to the scene.

After giving statements, and Glenn receives medical attention (if still alive), police search for the siblings. Assuming they got away and summoned the Windwalker, they are nowhere to be found; the police put out a call statewide to arrest the pair on sight. The Westhaven police are specifically notified.

- Talk of “stickmen” monsters gets quickly dismissed as the products of frightened teenage minds.
- If scout-investigators injured, captured, or killed Gustaf and Sydney, what do they tell the police? Assuming they claim “self-defense,” the police take the scouts for further questioning, and contact parents. After a few hours, scouts are released to their parents. While they may have to testify later, the scout-investigators are not blamed; in fact, they are heralded in the press.

Following any police involvement, the scoutinvestigators are either driven home (in the repaired bus) by a police officer or by their parents. Somehow, the scoutinvestigators return to Westhaven, where all is not well.

**Keeper note:** nothing further results of the police action. Westhaven Police visit Gustaf’s Farm, but find no sign of either sibling.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** move on to **Part Two: Hands of Winter** (following).

### Part Two: Hands of Winter

When the scout-investigators return to Westhaven, the situation has drastically changed for the worse. A shadow has fallen over the town. Impossibly, it’s in the grip of winter, and townsfolk have become weird.

Drawing close to Westhaven, the weather deteriorates. The sky grows gray, and the temperature plummets; the roadside foliage is adorned with hoar-frost.

- If scouts made their own way back to Westhaven, go to **First Impressions** (following).
- If scouts rode back with police or parents, go to **No Place Like Home** (page 151).

**Keeper note:** the town is under a curse that pervades every adult’s heart. Many serve **Thomas Cline** (page 60) without knowing why, while the rest cower in fear.

### First Impressions

Arriving in town, the streets are almost deserted. The front window of **Harley’s Sweet Shoppe** (page 56) is shattered, and glass shards litter the sidewalk. A kid’s bicycle lies abandoned in the street. An old man calls from a siding, “ _Go home, kids. Take shelter! Storm’s a comin’!”_

- If they wish to speak to someone on the street, pick an NPC from Chapter 3 ** or someone they know well, such as a **Trusted Adult** (page 25). The person acts distracted and fearful and says the following: _“You best get home. Not safe for you to be on the streets in this weather. The situation here is changing. Yes, changing. Go on now, get home!”_

The person keeps looking over their shoulder, as if scared of being watched. Despite their questions, the scout-investigators go unanswered, as the person doesn’t seem to hear them or is purposefully ignoring them. Suddenly, the person says they must get to work and hurries away.

**Keeper note:** if parents, guardians, or police officers drove the scouts back, they go straight home (see **No Place Like Home** , following), so this street encounter can happen later once the scout-investigators regroup.


### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** if they follow the person to whom they were talking, proceed to the **Signal Tower** (nearby).
- **Obvious:** if they want to go home, see **No Place Like Home** (following).

### No Place Like Home

On returning home, something is clearly wrong. For each scout-investigator, their house feels chilly (the fire is out). Their family members sit around, huddled in blankets, unspeaking.

armed men barge in, extinguish the fire, and dole out physical punishment to the home’s oldest male (see **Cline’s Henchmen** , page 162). If the scoutinvestigator interferes, they are targeted for a single attack (a slap or pushed on to the floor)—they can Dodge or fight back, but the henchmen gang up and automatically throw the scout to the floor and quickly leave again.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** mentioning the work crews or tower lead the scout-investigators to a **Signal Tower** (following).
- Whatever they say, the scout-investigator receives a lukewarm welcome and response. Everyone ignores them, and no family wants to talk.
- At least one family member is absent, helping a crew build a **Signal Tower** (nearby). Another family member whispers, _“They got sent to join a work crew to build a tower. Stay away from there!”_
- Mention of a fire brings fear to their faces. Someone
- says, “ _Mr. Cline banned fires.”_
- If a fire is made, smoke is spotted rising from the chimney, soon, a knock comes at the door. Three

### Signal Tower

Around town, numerous wooden towers rise, constructed by compliant townspeople. The towers resemble an oversized signal tower; as Wayfarer Scouts, the scout-investigators learned to build such towers.

A dozen or so townsfolk construct each tower, cutting and hauling timber. Shouts, hammering, and sawing can be heard upon approaching a site. Getting nearer, choose a scout-investigators’ Trusted Adult to be part of the crew.


### Chapter 7

### Adjusting for Arkham

If relocating the Westhaven Campaign to Arkham, this final part can be played without much change. Apart from using Arkham rather than Westhaven locations, events proceed as described. Arkham has fallen under Thomas Cline’s spell, and it’s up to the scout-investigators to save the day.

If the scout-investigators succeed in stopping Cline, events play out the same. The fine people of Arkham, just as with the folks of Westhaven, have encountered something otherworldly and bizarre— but the strangeness of the Mythos works weirdly on the human mind, and, in this case, causes everyone to forget what took place. Life quickly returns to normal, and only the scout-investigators (and any surviving cultists) remember what really happened.

If the scouts fail to stop Cline, assume he departs to pursue some grander scheme, allowing Arkham to return to normal. The Keeper has a new villain in Cline who can reappear later to trouble both the young scout-investigators and Arkham.

- The adults focus on their work and nothing else— unless it gets in their way or threatens the tower. This makes it easy get close to the workers.

### Worker Conflict

If a crew member realizes someone is actively working against them, such as attacking or restraining a worker, or sabotaging the tower, they yell and point at the wrongdoer; at this point, the other crew stop, mechanically turning as one, to stare at the interloper.

- Crew equal to the number of scout-investigators take whatever action seems appropriate to drive off or neutralize the threat. Use the **Westhaven Townsperson** profile (page 162) for work crew, adjusting for any specific NPCs as appropriate.
- The Trusted Adult warns the scouts, _“Mr. Cline will hear about this—then you’ll be in trouble! You best go directly to see Mr. Cline at the old Brumsted house and beg forgiveness!”_

**Keeper note:** the signal towers are mostly set dressing— destroying a signal tower may feel like a victory, but it is only a minor setback to Cline’s master plan. During


### Shadow over Westhaven

the climax of the scenario, people climb the signal towers and call out to the Windwalker. If the ceremony goes ahead (see **Brumsted’s Ascension** , page 156), the Windwalker appears. Those atop the towers are drawn bodily into the sky; some are never seen again, while the frozen corpses of others fall across the state, as if dropped from a great height.

### _**leads**_

- **Obvious:** finding Thomas Cline is easy; the Trusted Adult sends them to Gustaf’s Farm ( **Westhaven Map** , feature no. 18, page 54), south of town.


### Gustaf’s Farm

**Keeper note:** this is the climax of this scenario and The Westhaven Campaign. No more leads remain. For guidance on **Running the Final Scene** , see page 158.

Gustaf’s Farm (Westhaven Map, feature no. 18, page 54) stands alone south of Westhaven; a large, two-story building, with a steep roof. It was once grand, but now, peeling paint and missing shingles make it look neglected. Tall weeds, trash, and abandoned machinery fill the yard. A push lawnmower rusts by the tree-line, while a shiny black car is parked out front.

Townsfolk gather in the overgrown grounds at the rear of the house, where a wooden tower stands. It looks new; constructed of rough timber, it stands 9 yards/meters high. A ladder leads to a platform up top. Four unlit bonfires stand ready, some 6 yards/ meters away, around the platform.

- Scout-investigators may recognize Cline’s car—the same one that delivered Colonel Grimm’s package in **Box of Evil** (page 88). One of **Cline’s Henchmen** (page 162) is asleep in the driver’s seat. The glovebox contains a **Cellar Room** door-key (page 154).
- The crowd are under the same spell as the work crews. They do not act against the scout-investigators unless they visibly attempt to enter the house, sabotage the tower, or act against Cline’s plan.
- Scout-investigators may approach the other three sides of the house, out of the crowd’s view.
- Scouts can safely mix with the crowd, so long as they keep a low profile.
- Any scout-investigators peering through the windows see a dimly lit house full of ancient bric-a-brac, baroque furniture, shelves full of odd collections, and creepy paraphernalia. The few basement windows are boarded up from the inside, offering no glimpse inside.

### Entering the House

- Using the main entrance is difficult without being seen.
- A group **Stealth** roll can allow access to the main entrance without being seen.
- Another option is to cause a distraction.
- A successful **Locksmith** or Hard **Mechanical Repair** roll can open the locked front door quietly. A successful Hard **STR** roll can break through noisily, summoning the dozing henchman (see **Cline’s Henchmen** , page 162).
- Entering through a window is easier, requiring only a successful **STR** or **DEX** roll and a knife (or similar tool).

### Exploring Gustaf’s House

Inside, the air feels frigid, and everyone’s breath becomes frosty clouds.

### Kitchen

The kitchen is used and filthy. A foul-smelling haunch of meat sits on the table, surrounded by dead flies and maggots.


### Chapter 7

### Living Room

Filled with assorted old furniture. Piles of cobwebbed books are stacked haphazardly, an array of polished skulls line an entire bookshelf, and moldering nautical apparel lie thrown in a corner. Two single beds are covered in dirty sheets and blankets.

Piles of ragged clothes (male and female), as well as discarded food cartons, and several well-thumbed copies of the magazine _Love Story_ sit beside the beds. A substantial stack of firewood sits beside a dark fireplace.

- A casual search reveals an impromptu nest: a collection of broken furniture and smashed crates, gathered for a makeshift bed. Foul and nasty, the area has a thick coating of some greasy substance. Two **Wicker Ones** (page 162) use this space as a den; if disturbed, they attack, but do not pursue anyone out of the attic—remember, they have one penalty die on their combat rolls during daylight. At night, the wicker ones prey on the surrounding area.

### Basement

**Keeper note:** Gustaf and Sydney lived in this room until recently. The _Love Story_ magazines belong to Sydney. No books are present of any interest—unless the Keeper wishes to add one for a future plot seed.

High stacks of old newspapers and magazines form corridors around and through the space. A crying emanates from nearby (see **Cellar Room** , following).

### Cellar Room

### Other Ground Floor Rooms

Thick layers of dust and grime cover everything. Damp climbs the walls, and patches of plaster have fallen from the ceiling. Unnoted rooms are bereft of clues and of little interest to scout-investigators.

### Second Floor

The roof’s poor condition means rainwater has caused significant damage to the property. The stairs look rotten and treacherous.

- Climbing the stairs requires a successful **DEX** or **Climb** roll: failure inflicts 1D2 damage as the scoutinvestigator crashes through a rotten step.

All bedrooms are in similar repair: old bedframes are covered in fallen plaster; rat and bird nests abound.

- If scout-investigators persist in searching, Keepers may call for a **DEX** roll to avoid falling through rotten floorboards (1D4 damage; a successful **Jump** roll halves this).

### Attic

Filled with boxes and crates, the attic is one contiguous space, dark and foreboding. A labyrinth of unused furniture and stored goods, numerous holes gape in both the roof and the floor.

- Moving around safely can be accomplished by treading on the wooden joists; however, those moving at speed must succeed with a **DEX** roll or crash through to a bedroom below (1D4 damage; a successful **Jump** roll can halve this).

A crying comes from this room, but the heavy oak door is locked.

- A successful **Locksmith** or Hard **Mechanical Repair** roll can open the door; otherwise, it can be forced using a sturdy lever and a successful Hard **STR** roll.
- Several prisoners are shackled to the walls of this small, windowless room. The Keeper can decide the prisoners’ identity—use townspeople that the scout-investigators know from previous scenarios. At least one should be a scout-investigator’s Trusted Adult or a family member.
- If scout-investigators cannot open the door, they can still talk to those within, and perhaps promise to rescue them later.
- The door key is in the glovebox of Cline’s car that’s parked outside.
- Any scout-investigators captured earlier can be found here.

**Keeper note:** Cline later orders the building burned down, spelling death for any remaining prisoners. The scout-investigators must make a heroic return to save such souls from the flames.

### Laboratory

Contains stacked books and parchments, instruments (arcane and torturous), eerie spell components, and shelves of beakers, boxes, ceramic bowls, metal balls, philters, and vials. On a bloodstained table rests a plate with a fresh, half-eaten ham sandwich. An old coffin lies open on the floor.

- Refer to **Running the Final Scene** (page 158): this room may be vacant, or Cline may be here if **Raising Brumsted** (following).


### Chapter 7

### Disrupting the Spell

### Raising Brumsted

Brumsted’s coffin was dug up at Muskrat Lake and moved to Gustaf’s Farm. Cline uses **Reanimation** (page 171) to bring Brumsted to a semblance of life. The spell calls for a recently dead corpse, and this is not the case, as Brumsted’s corpse is old. The spell still works, though, but it’s short-lived, and when reanimated, Brumsted’s movements are jerky and stiff.

- For six hours leading up to the ritual, Cline is ensconced in the laboratory, licking every inch of Brumsted’s withered corpse (as the spell requires). So, when the scout-investigators arrive, Cline is probably here.
- Encountered here, Cline orders the scouts to leave and threatens to “tear and render the scouts’ flesh.” If pressed, he prematurely reanimates Brumsted ( **Disrupting the Spell** , following).

Should the scout-investigators try to disrupt the spell, Cline attacks or prematurely triggers Brumsted’s reanimation, taking one round to accomplish. Should he do this, Brumsted’s movement rate is reduced to 6, and his hit points halved to 5. See profiles for **Thomas Cline** (page 60) and the reanimated **Jedediah Brumsted** (page 135) in **Characters & Monsters** (page 163).

### Brumsted’s Ascension

**Keeper note:** the following scene is described as Cline intends—change it if the scout-investigators act at any point.

Read aloud or paraphrase the following:

> _No announcement occurs; the crowd just falls silent as if instructed. Some sit, others stand, and some hold their hands aloft and chant. Cline and the reanimated Brumsted emerge from the house. The crowd cheers jubilantly and parts. Bonfires start to dance with flames. Cline climbs the ladder to the top, followed somewhat stiffly by Brumsted._


### Shadow over Westhaven


> _Atop the platform, Cline raises his hands, and the crowd falls silent. He then speaks:_

“At last, the hour is upon us! Windwalker, hear me. I adjure you to accept your loyal servant, Jedediah Brumsted, once dead, now risen. Let his bond with you be reforged. Accept Brother Brumsted into your embrace, and accept me, Thomas Cline, as your humble servant.”

> _Thunder cracks, and a strong wind circles the platform. Clouds roil and part. A vast nightmare figure—the Windwalker—manifests in the sky. The Windwalker is humanoid, with bony, elongated, withered features formed of gray cloud. Its eyes shine down like twin yellow stars._

### **•** Ask for a **cool** Roll When the Windwalker Appears.

> _The crowd panics. Some flee screaming, some faint, while others fall to their knees in frenzied worship. Brumsted rises vertically from the platform, high into the sky, and disappears. The clouds roll closed, and the sky darkens. Then, a single pillar-like shaft of light beams down from the sky beside the platform. Cline steps off the platform and into the beam, but instead of plummeting to the ground, he is suspended in the light. Arms stretched wide, head tilted back, his voice booms out:_

“Yea, master, I will not disappoint. Iä, Iä, praise the Windwalker! All hail!” _The remnants of the crowd echo in unison,_ “Hail!”

_Cline now descends smoothly to the ground. The beam of light fades as Cline’s feet touch the earth. Cline looks to what remains of his loyal followers, saying,_ “We have work to do.” _Cline waves a hand toward the mansion,_ “Burn it down; I have no further use of it.”

With that, Cline heads to his car and drives into town, leaving the mob to torch the platform and house.

### Dealing with the Crowds

As noted, a crowd gathered for Brumsted’s ascension.

### _**before the Windwalker Appears**_

If the scout-investigators act against Brumsted or Cline prior to the Windwalker’s arrival, the crowd tries to stop them.

- Each round, a **Westhaven Townsperson** (page 162) attempts to grab each scout-investigator and restrain them—resolve using a combat maneuver.
  - -investigators get released in the ensuing chaos. Those who stick around may be attacked again.
- If restrained, the scout-investigator is simply held and forced to witness proceedings. Weapons are confiscated.
- Each round, ask the scout-investigators for a **Luck** roll: failure means they are targeted by a **Westhaven Townsperson** (page 162), who attempts to restrain or attack them (Keeper’s discretion).

### _**after the Windwalker Appears**_

When the Windwalker appears, restrained scout
### Defeating Brumsted

Brumsted’s sole aim is to climb the platform and ascend to be with the Windwalker. If anyone stands in his way, he attacks them. Brumsted can no longer wield magic, but he can make physical attacks. Should he be reduced to zero hit points prior to ascension, he falls lifeless to the floor and a light begins to appear in his chest (see **Howl of the Windwalker** , following).

### Howl of the Windwalker

On Brumsted’s “death,” a bright yellow light glows from his heart, while a swirling, ice-blue, magical Gate opens behind him. Within seconds, the Gate expands to 10 feet (3 m) wide, and a violent wind begins to blow into the Gate. The howl is deafening—the howl of the Windwalker! Through the Gate, scout-investigators see the terrifying icy wastes the Windwalker calls home. Brumsted’s remains are sucked through and scattered across the ice, into oblivion.

- Some crowd members get swept up by the wind and blown into the Gate; others leap through willingly, ecstatically praising the Windwalker’s name as they go.
- Scout-investigators know to keep away from the Gate and, ideally, grab hold of something to avoid being swept in. Call for a **STR** roll to resist being pulled through the Gate—if they seize something solid, grant one bonus die.
- After one round, the Gate closes.

**Keeper note:** if any scout-investigators are swept through the Gate, consider delaying the Gate’s closure a few seconds. This allows time for attempts to save their friend; perhaps they hold hands and form a chain or find a rope to help and pull them free just as the Gate closes. If the scouts work together, no roll is needed. However, the Keeper may require the unfortunate scout-investigator to spend their Luck to return, using the **Cheating Death** rule (page 42).

### Defeating Cline

Cline is prepared to do anything to protect Brumsted. He has several spells at his command, see **Thomas Cline’s** profile (page 60). He can use **Enthrall Victim** (page 170) if he faces more than one or two targets or wishes a more subtle effect. As an overt attack, he can employ **Tongue of the Woods** (page 172).

### Cline Blessed by the Windwalker

After descending to the ground on a beam of light (see **Brumsted’s Ascension** , page 156), Cline’s power receives a boost from the Windwalker’s blessing:

- His POW increases to 90 (an additional 6 magic points).

- **Armor:** 1-point; Cline’s skin hardens and becomes bluish.

- His STR increases to 75 and his SIZ to 70 (Build increases to 1, damage bonus to +1D4, and hit points to 12).

- **Piercing Cold Attack:** Cline can now inflict freezing damage. If he grabs a target with a combat maneuver, each round following where he maintains his frigid grip, he inflicts 1D3+1 damage as he saps his target’s body heat. Those losing half their hit points to this attack must make a **CON** roll: failure leads to frostbite and permanent loss of 1D10 CON.

### Running the Final Scene

All the various elements are here—the creepy house, the crowd outside, head-cultist Cline, undead Brumstead, and the ritual. But how the scene unfolds depends on the scout-investigators’ approach to this point and how you choose to handle those pieces. Some possible ways appear below as guidance.

### Arriving Early

The crowd is starting to gather outside the house as the scout-investigators arrive.

- If they wait and watch, Cline and Brumsted come out and the ritual begins—play it out as described in **Arriving in the Nick of Time** (following).
- If scout-investigators break into the house, they may confront Cline in the laboratory while he is **Raising Brumsted** (page 156). This probably results in a fight against Cline and the prematurely reanimated Brumsted.

- One option is to have some of Cline’s Henchmen (page 162) come to Cline’s aid; in which case, they aim to capture, rather than kill, the scout-investigators, who find themselves forced to watch the ceremony. As described in **Brumsted’s Ascension** (page 156), when


> — SHADOW OVER WESTHAVEN

the Windwalker appears, the crowd panics and the scout-investigators regain their freedom—prior to this, consider allowing those with **Knot-Tying Badges** (page 32) to automatically free themselves, and/or a Hard **STR** or **DEX** roll to free their bonds.

### Arriving in the Nick of Time

The scout-investigators arrive just as Cline and Brumsted exit the house to commence their ritual (see **Brumsted’s Ascension** , page 156). Consider this the simpler, default ending; it plays out as described in the preceding text, with the onus on players to stop events progressing too far.

### Evening the Odds

The scout-investigators look to be outmatched, but they do have some things that can help:

- See **Boons** (page 159) for a list of items that they may be able to use.

- The optional Luck Spending rule (page 41) gives players the chance to adjust poor skill rolls, and the **Cheating Death** rule (page 42) can save scoutinvestigators from certain death.

### Gustaf & Sydney Reappear

If the Windwalker scooped these two up from the **Woodland Altar** (page 143) at Muskrat Lake, it could, optionally, deposit them where and when the Keeper wishes—to either thwart or aid the scout-investigators:

- Should the scout-investigators be losing, introduce Gustaf and Sydney to wreak revenge on Cline for double-crossing them.
- If the scout-investigators are winning too easily, introduce Gustaf and Sydney to aid Cline; or perhaps they supplant him, taking over the ritual to gain the Windwalker’s blessing for themselves.
- For a straightforward approach, assume Gustaf and Sydney were consumed by the Windwalker, never to be seen again.

**Keeper note:** feel free to ignore these suggestions and come up with your own.

### Timing

The timing of attacks against Brumsted and Cline is important:

- If they wait until _after_ Brumsted’s ascension, they have only _one_ adversary (Cline); however, soon after Brumsted’s ascension, Cline receives the Windwalker’s blessing, and his powers increase.

### Boons

Over the course of the Westhaven Campaign scenarios, the scout-investigators had opportunities to collect some or all the following items and spells:

### _**baba Itzak’s Broach (from Treasure of the Secret Way)**_

**Baba Itzak’s Broach** (page 114) helps if targeted by Cline’s spells, granting the wearer one bonus die on opposed POW rolls to negate his spells.

### _**the Black Stone (from Box of Evil)**_

The Black Stone (page 105) has no power against Brumsted or Cline; but if that pesky **Homunculus** (page 111) shows up (which it can, assuming it survived, if the Keeper wishes), then it still works against the creature.

### _**bless Blade Spell (from Tremors Below)**_

With **Bless Blade** (page 170), they could have created one or more blessed blades, which ignore Brumsted and Cline’s armor.

### _**dust of Suleiman (from Box of Evil)**_

Should they still possess any of this valuable powder, it may prove effective. While **Dust of Suleiman** (page 103) inflicts no damage to humans, it can affect Brumsted; he is now something other than human and suffers half damage (1D10) from the dust.

If Cline becomes blessed by the Windwalker, he also becomes something other than human and can also suffer half damage (1D10) from the dust.

### _**the Ghost of Strauss (from Treasure of the Secret Way)**_

If scout-investigators used the Banish the Visitor (page 170) to trap **Heinrich Strauss’ Ghost** (page 131), they may still possess the glass bottle containing Strauss’ essence. If released now, the ghost can enact its final revenge.

When the cork stopper is removed or the bottle broken, the ghost appears, and if Brumsted is nearby, it floats toward him smiling. Brumsted cowers, as ghost Strauss raises his phantom pickaxe one last time and buries it in Brumsted’s heart, killing him. With that, Strauss dances a few steps and disappears forever.

- If scout-investigators act _before_ Brumsted’s ascension, they must confront _two_ adversaries: Brumsted and Cline.


### Chapter 7


### _**immolate Spell (from Box of Evil)**_

**Immolate** (page 171) works against Brumsted and Cline (1D6 damage)—but the fire goes out after one round.

### _**totem (from Tremors Below)**_

The protective **Totem** (page 171) created to ward off the Burrowing Assassin, or one like it, has a limited effect against Brumsted and Cline; it keeps each of them away 1D3 rounds, after which they ignore the effect.

### Conclusion

If all goes well, the scout-investigators saved Westhaven, and the shadow is dispelled. The town returns to normal. People go about their business and quickly forget what took place. While no one understands why, the townsfolk now treat the scout-investigators differently, more like equals. Passing by Harley’s Sweet Shoppe, Bob is replacing a windowpane and greets them warmly. The council president, Mr. Booker, thanks them most sincerely, and adds that he hopes he can rely on their votes.

Only their old friend Boxcar Jim is nowhere to be seen.

Despite this general memory loss, the scout-investigators remember what happened: do they keep this a secret among themselves?

**Keeper note:** if the scout-investigators failed to stop Cline, he remains in charge, the townsfolk still under his spell. Thus, Westhaven develops a foul reputation. The overt horrors hide in the shadows, and the town returns to a semblance of normality; but, behind closed doors, townsfolk live in fear. Who knows what nefarious plans Cline may have… _Mr. James_
### Memories of Boxcar Jim

As an added twist, should Keepers wish, the scoutinvestigators meet **Boxcar Jim** (page 62) one more time. Rather than his usual rags, Jim sports a casual suit and an armful of groceries. His hair is cut short, and he looks quite smart. When addressed, he looks confused and shakes his head and speaks perfect English with a well-bred New England accent:

> _“I’m sorry, I think you have me confused with someone else. My name is James Cavendish. Only my mother ever called me Jim. Now, if you’ll excuse me, my wife expects me for dinner.”_
